## Piece 45: LOOM — A Ferroelectric Crystal Weaves Its Own Domains Like Cloth (2026-08-13)
This session's web wander ("novel physics discovery August 2026" and
"new mathematics discovery 2026 geometry pattern visualization") surfaced
several candidates -- a woven fabric structure inside a crystal, a
150-year-old geometric rule (Bonnet, already used by this gallery's own
BONNET piece) shown wrong for a second, independent construction, a
nanoparticle superlattice freezing an intermediate crystal phase -- and
went with the first: on 6 August 2026 (one week before this session ran),
Light: Science & Applications published "Spontaneous formation and
optical manipulation of a woven domain fabric in a ferroelectric
crystal" (Feifei Xin, Eugenio Del Re, Aharon J. Agranat et al. -- Sapienza
Univ. Rome, Nankai Univ., Hebrew Univ. of Jerusalem, Univ. of Groningen;
DOI 10.1038/s41377-026-02374-7). WebFetch reached the ARTICLE ITSELF, not
just an abstract (Light: Sci. Appl. is gold open access) -- confirmed
independently against phys.org's and bioengineer.org's coverage, all
three agreeing on authors, DOI, material and the central claim. Checked
against this gallery's own memory before committing (grep for
"ferroelectric|woven|weave|KTN|linking number" across every existing
piece and this file): BRAID already renders literal knots/links, but from
eigenvalue monodromy around a non-Hermitian exceptional point in a
2-parameter drive space, not a real spatial lattice structure -- a
genuinely different mechanism and a genuinely different visual (a woven
textile, not a braided ribbon), so this cleared the novelty bar.

The physical claim itself: a specially grown potassium-lithium-tantalate-
niobate crystal (K0.997Ta0.62Nb0.38O3:Li0.003, "KTN:Li"/KLTN, Curie point
Tc = 292 K measured), grown with periodic compositional striations (5 um
period), develops ferroelectric domains on cooling that do not form the
ordinary parallel stripes every other ferroelectric shows -- they weave,
crossing over and under each other in 3D on the striation grating's own
10 um lattice constant (exactly twice the growth striation period). The
authors quantify each crossing with a knot-theory linking number, Lk=+1
for over, Lk=-1 for under, and report the sign is HISTORY-DEPENDENT:
re-cooling the same crystal weaves a genuinely different pattern at the
same lattice sites. Real woven cloth alternates over/under with perfect
deterministic regularity; this crystal instead freezes in a quenched,
DISORDERED weave -- the "novel and unexpected" hook this session's
routine explicitly asks the web wander to look for.

What is real and was extracted from the article and reproduced exactly:
the 10 um lattice constant (= 2x the 5 um growth striation period), the
292 K Curie point, the narrow reported formation window (-2.0 K > T-Tc >
-8.0 K during slow cooling, alpha < 0.4 K/min -- weave-specific, not just
"below Tc"), the four depths the authors actually imaged (z = 0, -8,
-16, -24 um, multifocus white-light optical microscopy, ~0.8-0.9 um
resolution), the Lk sign convention itself, and the laser-rewriting
numbers (a focused 514 nm beam, intensity 6.3e6 W/cm^2, 0.5 um step,
generating photoinduced space-charge fields above 200 V/mm from deep
in-band impurities that screen the charged domain walls' bound charge
enough to let them locally migrate and disentangle -- the paper reports
1040 nm infrared light does nothing, consistent with an above-bandgap or
deep-trap photoexcitation mechanism rather than simple heating).

What is NOT the paper's own data, and is disclosed as such on screen and
in the piece's own header: the actual per-crossing Lk pattern. Nothing
WebFetch reached gives a formula, a measured spatial correlation length,
or even a measured +/- ratio for the disorder -- only that it is
"history-dependent" and (from the described micrographs, not seen by
this session) visibly patchy rather than independent per site. So the
piece stands in with a 3D value-noise field (hash-based, trilinear-
interpolated) thresholded at each crossing to assign Lk. Verified in Node
before any canvas code (verify_noise.js, this session's scratchpad, not
committed): at the frequency the piece actually uses (0.25 lattice
units), a 40x40 sample came out 50.1% positive (essentially unbiased) and
88.5% same-sign agreement between adjacent crossings (patchy, not
salt-and-pepper) -- confirming the noise field behaves the way the
piece's own header claims, but the 88.5% correlation length itself is
this piece's own aesthetic tuning, not a number measured from the
crystal. A second, independent noise channel gives each crossing a random
"nucleation threshold" so the weave visibly crystallizes crossing-by-
crossing as the formation window is swept through, rather than switching
on everywhere simultaneously -- also an invented, if physically-
motivated, dramatization.

Three modes, tested in a real headless browser (Playwright, environment's
preinstalled Chromium; zero console/page errors across all three modes,
plus drag interaction, a sustained laser-paint gesture, and 1280x800
desktop + 390x844 mobile screenshots). WEAVE: drag X sweeps T-Tc from
+3 K (paraelectric, blank) down through the real -2/-8 K window (crossings
visibly nucleate crossing-by-crossing) to fully woven and holding --
holding, not reverting, past -8 K, since nothing in the reachable text
says the weave un-forms on further cooling and this piece declined to
invent a reversal; drag Y moves the viewing depth z continuously through
the crystal, each depth an independently-correlated noise slice, sharing
a small dot-marker axis with the four real measured depths. QUENCH:
replays the cooling sweep on a timer, re-seeding the noise field fresh
every cycle, so the SAME lattice visibly weaves a DIFFERENT pattern every
run -- the piece's most literal illustration of "history-dependent
disorder" as an observed event rather than a caption. LASER: a fixed
mid-window weave a visitor can drag across with a 514 nm glow to locally
untangle (the crossing's under-dip is suppressed once its accumulated
"written" amount crosses 0.5, echoing the real threshold-like screening
mechanism); a quick tap reheats and erases the whole written zone.

### Honest gaps, disclosed on screen and in the header
- Only ONE z-slice is ever rendered at a time -- never the true
  simultaneous 3D interleave across all four measured depths at once,
  the same single-projection simplification nearly every other 3D-
  structure piece in this gallery (CAUSTIC, BONNET, NOPERTHEDRON) already
  discloses for its own subject.
- No Ginzburg-Landau or free-energy derivation of WHY the striation
  grating induces weaving rather than plain stripes was attempted --
  unlike CAUSTIC (Christoffel eigenproblem) or MAELSTROM (a governing PDE
  solved and verified before any shader code), this piece's crossing
  pattern is a disclosed noise-field stand-in, not a first-principles
  simulation, because the reachable text supplies no order parameter or
  formula to solve.
- The real paper's own +/- Lk ratio and spatial correlation length were
  never found (not stated in the reachable article text, or missed by
  this session's read of it) -- the 50/50 balance and 88.5% neighbor
  correlation used here are this piece's own tuned aesthetic choice,
  verified only for internal consistency, not fit to measured data.
- LASER mode's untangle threshold (accumulate to 0.5 via a Gaussian
  brush, 0.18 per full-strength dab) is an invented interaction constant,
  not derived from the paper's real exposure/step numbers (1 s exposure,
  0.5 um step, 6.3e6 W/cm^2), which are quoted on screen for their real
  values but not used to derive the erase rate itself.

### Inspiration for a future session
Passed over again, still unpursued: the Sylvester-Gallai conjecture's
reported first AI-only proof (LeanMind, IAS/DeepMind, Coq-verified) and
the matrix-multiplication exponent omega dropping below 2.3727 via
tensor decomposition + deep RL, both first flagged by session 44's own
web wander and not chased by this session either; a wandering
supermassive black hole exposed by a star's tidal disruption 30,000
light-years from its host galaxy's center (Univ. of Maryland, Aug 2026),
flagged the same session, likewise still open. New this session: the
Brown/Michigan nanoparticle superlattice stabilizing an elusive
intermediate phase between two common metallic crystal structures
(surfaced by the same "novel physics discovery" search that found LOOM's
own woven-crystal thread, not investigated further this session), and
the paper's own title -- "...and OPTICAL MANIPULATION of a woven domain
fabric" -- which this session's WebFetch confirmed includes real local
disentangling but did NOT confirm (or rule out) whether the authors also
demonstrate writing a CHOSEN new over/under sign at a target crossing,
as opposed to only erasing toward "plain"; this piece's LASER mode
only ever erases, matching what this session actually verified, not
what the title's "manipulation" might additionally cover -- a future
session reading the paper's own manipulation-protocol section in full
could confirm one way or the other and add targeted rewriting if real.

## Piece 44: CAUSTIC — Heat, Guided Like Light, Through a Crystal's Own Anisotropy (2026-08-12)
This session's web wander picked up a thread flagged and left unpursued
TWICE already: session 42's own inspiration note (see its Piece 42 entry
above) named "phonon ballistic heat rays at room temperature" among four
candidates surfaced by a general "novel 2025/2026 discovery" search, went
with MURMURATION instead; session 43 (BILLOW) picked solar vortices from
a near-identical candidate pool the very next day. This session searched
again ("UCLA heat wave-like rays crystal room temperature August 2026
phonon") and confirmed it: Man Li, Huan Wu, Zihao Qin, Chuanjin Su, Huu
Duy Nguyen & Yongjie Hu (UCLA Samueli) published, in Nature Physics on 23
July 2026 (DOI 10.1038/s41567-026-03335-y, confirmed live against UCLA's
own newsroom release and an independent SciTechDaily summary, which agree
on authors, DOI, and the claim that different crystal faces of boron
arsenide show distinct sixfold/eightfold/fourfold focusing patterns), the
first ROOM-TEMPERATURE observation of phonon focusing -- heat-carrying
lattice vibrations traveling in "focused, ray-like paths" rather than
diffusing, guided by the crystal's own elastic anisotropy. The mechanism
itself (Northrop & Wolfe, 1980; Every, 1981) had only ever been
photographed at cryogenic temperatures before; BAs's own unusually weak
phonon scattering -- the same property behind its record ~1300 W/mK
thermal conductivity -- is what lets the effect survive up to 300K. The
Nature Physics article itself sits behind a subscription wall and was NOT
read by this session -- see the caveat below on what that means for this
piece's honesty.

Checked against this gallery's own memory before committing (grep for
"phonon|christoffel|elastic constant|caustic|slowness surface|group
velocity" across every existing piece): TKACHENKO mentions phonons only
as an analogy for superfluid Kelvin waves, and nothing in the gallery
touches elastic anisotropy, the Christoffel wave equation, or ballistic
imaging at all -- a genuinely new phenomenon AND a new piece of
mathematical machinery (a 3x3 tensor eigenproblem) for this gallery.

Rather than redraw UCLA's own (unread) micrograph, this piece SOLVES the
actual mechanism from scratch for the actual material, using numbers
already on the public record: the cubic elastic stiffness tensor of BAs,
measured by the SAME lab five years earlier (Kang, Li, Wu, Nguyen & Hu,
"Basic Physical Properties of Cubic Boron Arsenide," Appl. Phys. Lett.
115, 122103, 2019 -- fetched and read in full via WebFetch, not just
summarized) -- C11=285 GPa, C12=79.5 GPa, C44=149 GPa, rho=5.22 g/cm^3 --
fed into the Christoffel wave equation, which is literally that 2019
paper's own equation 9: [k^2 l_i l_j c_ijkl - rho*omega^2 delta_ik][U_i]=0.
A closed-form (trigonometric) eigensolver for real symmetric 3x3 matrices
was written and verified in Node (verify_christoffel.js, this session's
scratchpad, not committed) against that SAME 2019 paper's own MEASURED
sound velocities before a single pixel was drawn: v_L[100] computed 7389.0
m/s vs their measured 7390; v_T[100] computed 5342.7 vs measured 5340;
v_L[111] computed 8149.3 vs their measured 8150 -- agreement to the
reported precision on all three, from the three published stiffness
constants alone, no fitting. Group velocity (the direction phonon ENERGY
actually travels, as opposed to the wavevector direction) was computed by
numerically differentiating the dispersion relation omega(k) via central
finite differences (verify_groupvel.js) and checked the same way: exactly
zero walk-off at every high-symmetry direction ([100],[110],[111], as
required by mirror symmetry), rising smoothly to nonzero values in
between (up to ~8.6 degrees for the quasi-longitudinal branch scanned
across the full [100]-to-[110] arc) -- the qualitative shape phonon-
focusing theory predicts.

The rendering itself went through three failed attempts before landing on
one that worked, each diagnosed by actually looking at the output, not by
assuming the math was the problem: (1) a Monte Carlo scatter/histogram
with too few samples (3M) over a 500x500 output grid was pure shot noise,
no visible structure; (2) a DENSE deterministic input grid (2200x2200)
fixed the noise but introduced fine concentric Moire ripples from nearest-
bin quantization against the nonlinear group-velocity map; (3) switching
the SAME dense deterministic grid to bilinear (as opposed to nearest-bin)
splatting killed the Moire completely and revealed the real structure
underneath: the quasi-longitudinal branch stays smooth and undramatic
near [001] (no folds, matching its small measured walk-off), while the
two TRANSVERSE branches fold sharply -- FAST-T throws a bright cross along
the <100> mirror lines, SLOW-T throws a dramatic curved X-shaped caustic
with a small four-petal rosette at its center, and viewed along [111]
SLOW-T resolves into a clean three-fold trefoil, exactly the fold-count-
depends-on-viewing-face behavior the UCLA press materials describe
(qualitatively; this session did not have their actual images to compare
against pixel-for-pixel). This matches the long-known fact from germanium
and silicon ballistic-phonon imaging (Wolfe, "Imaging Phonons", 1998)
that transverse branches focus far more violently than the longitudinal
one -- now reproduced here specifically from BAs's own measured tensor.
The FINAL rendering technique, used live in the browser (not precomputed),
sidesteps the offline Moire/noise tradeoff entirely: thousands of random
wavevector directions are resampled every single frame and additively
splatted onto a slowly-fading accumulation buffer, so temporal averaging
across frames does the denoising a single static image would otherwise
need a deterministic grid for -- continuous, GPU-free, and it also means
the pattern responds live to the crystal orientation the visitor drags to.

GUIDE mode is the live accumulation described above (drag: x = azimuth
0-45deg exploiting cubic mirror symmetry, y = polar tilt 0-90deg from
[001]; click/tap cycles the three phonon branches; auto-drifts through the
orientation space when idle, same sinusoidal-phase technique as BILLOW's
own currentXY()). WALKOFF mode is a companion diagram, not just a second
skin: for a fan of wavevector directions swept through an EXACT mirror
plane (snapped to azimuth 0 or 45deg specifically so the diagram has zero
undisplayed out-of-plane component), it draws the dim wavevector spokes
against the bright group-velocity arrows side by side, letting a visitor
see directly why GUIDE mode goes bright wherever neighboring arrows
converge -- caught and fixed one real bug this session: the first version
called the shared buildView(phi,theta=0) helper to get a reference frame,
not realizing that at theta=0 the view axis w=[001] doesn't depend on phi
at all, so the diagram's fan plane silently ignored the drag input
entirely (two different mirror-plane drags produced pixel-identical
output, caught by literally diffing two screenshots, not by reading the
code). Fixed by constructing the fan's in-plane axis directly from phi
instead of reusing that helper.

Verified in a real headless browser (Playwright 1.56.1 against the
environment's preinstalled Chromium, /opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194):
zero console/page errors across both modes, all three branches, four
named crystal orientations ([001],[100]/[010],[110],[111], the last three
reached by direct dragX/dragY injection since natural mouse drag plus the
auto-drift phase reconstruction made landing exactly on a symmetry point
by hand unreliable), one 1280x800 desktop and one 390x844 mobile-portrait
screenshot.

Honest caveats, all from this session:
- This is the classical, non-dispersive elastic-continuum limit (omega
  linear in |k|): the ray DIRECTIONS shown are exact for BAs's measured
  elastic tensor, but real THz phonons eventually depart from this near
  the zone boundary, and this piece shows direction only, never distance
  or attenuation -- the paper's own headline claim (how FAR the ray-like
  regime survives before scattering) is not modeled at all.
- The three branches are labeled by eigenvalue ORDER (largest/mid/
  smallest), not by adiabatic continuity; at exact degeneracy loci the
  numeric branch label can hop discontinuously. Not resolved this session.
- Per-branch zoom in GUIDE mode (1.55x / 1.0x / 0.62x) is a display choice
  tuned by eye so all three branches fill the frame, not a physical
  quantity -- disclosed in the piece's own header comment.
- This session's own Every (1981) closed-form Gaussian-curvature
  enhancement factor was never derived or cross-checked against the
  Monte-Carlo accumulation actually used; they should agree in principle
  but this was not verified independently.
- The paper's own actual images (sixfold/eightfold/fourfold, per crystal
  FACE, i.e. a specific finite sample geometry) were never seen by this
  session; this piece instead sweeps a CONTINUOUS view axis over the full
  orientation sphere, which is the more general (and, this session
  believes, more honest) object, but is not literally the same picture
  UCLA published.

## Piece 43: BILLOW — Kelvin-Helmholtz Vortices at a Solar Granule's Edge (2026-08-11)
This session's web wander started broad ("novel unexpected scientific
discovery August 2026") and surfaced three candidates within days of each
other: quantum entanglement generated directly from sunlight (Ottawa/MPL,
Optica 2026), a reversed-direction jet in Earth's molten core, and tiny
plasma whirlpools resolved on the Sun's own surface for the first time.
The solar vortices won out: David Kuridze, Michiel van Noort, Friedrich
Woger, Sami K. Solanki and colleagues (NSF National Solar Observatory, Max
Planck Institute for Solar System Research, High Altitude Observatory)
published direct images, taken with the 4-meter NSF Daniel K. Inouye
Solar Telescope at 416nm, of vortices only ~20km wide swirling in the
lanes between solar granules (Nature, 5 August 2026, DOI
10.1038/s41586-026-10871-3, confirmed live by this session against two
independent secondary sources -- MPG's and ScienceDaily's own summaries,
which agreed on author list, DOI, and figures -- and against doi.org
itself, which correctly 302-redirects that DOI to a real nature.com
article page, though the article itself sits behind Nature's login and
was not read in full). Checked against this gallery's own memory: no
prior piece touches Kelvin-Helmholtz instability, solar granulation, or
magnetohydrodynamics at all (grep for "kelvin|helmholtz|granule" turns up
only TKACHENKO's unrelated Kelvin WAVES on a superfluid vortex lattice) --
a genuinely new phenomenon and a new physical setting (a star's own
convective skin) for this gallery, distinct from every earlier fluid
piece (EDDY's bluff-body vortex shedding, MAELSTROM's Aharonov-Bohm vortex
optics, TKACHENKO's quantized vortex lattice).

The team's own mechanism claim is textbook Kelvin-Helmholtz instability
(Helmholtz 1868, Kelvin 1871) at the shear boundary between adjacent
granules' oppositely-flowing plasma -- caught here for the first time
only because Inouye's own resolution (a 20km structure is comparable to
resolving a one-euro coin from 180km away) finally reached the scale
these vortices actually live at. The paper's own significance claim: the
vortices continuously and efficiently mix MAGNETIZED and NON-MAGNETIZED
plasma, a candidate mechanism for magnetic flux transport faster than
smooth diffusion, with knock-on relevance to nanoflares and possibly the
Sun's 11-year cycle.

ROLL mode is a real, from-scratch 2D incompressible-flow solver (semi-
Lagrangian velocity advection, Stam 1999, plus a pressure-projection step
enforcing zero divergence), seeded with a tanh shear profile and a small
sinusoidal nudge, with a passive "magnetized/non-magnetized" dye field
advected by the same velocity to show the mixing the paper's own language
emphasizes. Verified in Node before any canvas code (verify_kh.js,
debug_poisson2.js through debug_poisson5.js, this session's scratchpad,
not committed): the first, most natural collocated-grid implementation --
central differences for BOTH divergence and the pressure gradient --
silently fails to converge (a Jacobi solve's own Poisson RESIDUAL went
to ~0 while the actually-projected velocity field's divergence stayed
stuck near its pre-projection value, plateauing then slowly INCREASING
over more iterations); diagnosed as the classic checkerboard/odd-even
decoupling null space of same-stencil central differences on a collocated
grid (composing two stride-2 central-difference operators gives a
stride-2 second derivative, not the stride-1 five-point Laplacian the
Jacobi solve actually assumes). Fixed with a forward-difference divergence
paired with a backward-difference gradient (an exact adjoint pair whose
composition IS the standard five-point Laplacian) plus a Neumann
(zero-gradient) pressure condition at the two free-slip walls, applied as
a ghost-row copy refreshed every Jacobi sweep; re-verified after the fix,
max divergence after projection fell to <0.5% of its pre-projection
value (down from >45% stuck-and-rising with the broken version), and a
220-step run with a real perturbation rolled up with >2000x more
dye-interleaving variance near the interface than an unperturbed control
run through the identical solver -- a genuine positive-control /
negative-control pair, not just "it runs."

A second, separate tuning problem surfaced only once this ran in an
actual browser via Playwright screenshots: the originally-planned
"grid diffusion regularizes the classically-ill-posed zero-thickness
sheet" approach produced only a gentle, slowly-DECAYING sine wave at the
interface, never rolling into visible billows, even after hundreds of
simulated time units -- diagnosed in Node (tune_roll*.js, scratchpad) as
numerical diffusion from repeated bilinear resampling outpacing the
instability's own growth at the originally-chosen timestep/thickness.
Fixed by retuning (larger per-step dt with fewer total steps for a given
elapsed time -- since each semi-Lagrangian step smooths by roughly a
fixed sub-cell amount regardless of dt, FEWER steps means less
accumulated smoothing for the same physical time), a thinner seed layer,
and a larger perturbation amplitude, which together produce a genuine
rise-saturate-decay life cycle (checked across the full n=1..6 and
shear-strength drag range: peak dye-interleaving variance 0.07-0.17,
none stuck flat) that visibly rolls into the classic "cat's eye" billow
shape by mid-cycle before eventually smoothing back out -- an honestly
disclosed choice (the piece's own header says so directly): wall-clock
pacing is decoupled from the solver's own dt by throttling step
FREQUENCY (a fixed real-time interval between steps), not by changing the
solver's own timestep, so retuning playback speed never re-introduces the
diffusion problem the first fix solved.

THRESHOLD mode is the other half of the real physics the paper only
gestures at: derived here (not copied from a single quoted number) from
Chandrasekhar's general two-stream magnetized Kelvin-Helmholtz dispersion
relation ("Hydrodynamic and Hydromagnetic Stability," 1961) for the
symmetric equal-density, equal-field case: sigma(k) =
k*sqrt((deltaU/2)^2 - vA^2*cos^2(theta)), vA the Alfven speed, theta the
angle between the field and the perturbation's wavevector. Checked in
Node against four limiting cases before shipping (verify_kh.js's
testMHDDispersion): B=0 recovers the unconditional classical growth rate;
the symmetric aligned threshold sits at EXACTLY deltaU=2*vA (sigma^2=0 to
<1e-9); below that threshold sigma^2<0 (a neutrally stable, undamped
oscillation -- the piece's own UI first mislabeled this "decaying" before
a self-review caught that ideal inviscid MHD has no dissipation to decay
into, and fixed the copy and the plotted curve to show only the real
growth rate, clamped at zero, rather than a fabricated negative branch);
and theta=90 degrees (field perpendicular to the wavevector) gives
IDENTICAL growth to B=0 regardless of field strength, since only the
field's component along k resists that particular ripple -- a real,
useful resolution of why the paper's own vortices apparently appear
wherever the field is merely "sufficiently strong" rather than being
suppressed by it: a real photospheric field threading a granule boundary
is rarely exactly parallel to the local flow.

GRANULE mode is explicitly labeled illustrative, not simulated: a
procedural jittered-point nearest-neighbor granulation field (bright
centers, dark lanes, the real visual signature of granulation
photographs) at the paper's own 500-2000km scale (this piece uses
~1000km per cell), with swirl glyphs seeded at a procedural "field
strength" value along the lanes, drawn at the piece's own correct ~1:50
vortex:granule size ratio for whatever zoom level the visitor drags to --
at the wide view the glyphs are sub-pixel and invisible, exactly as these
vortices were to every solar telescope before Inouye's own resolution
reached them; zoomed to a single lane they resolve into visible spinning
glyphs, a direct dramatization of what "diffraction limit" meant here.

Open items for a future session: (a) ROLL mode's rise-then-decay cycle is
this session's own tuned choice, not a fit to Michalke's (1964) actual
tanh-profile eigenvalue calculation (fastest-growing wavelength ~7-7.7x
the shear-layer half-width) -- reproducing that specific published number
would need solving the Rayleigh stability equation as an eigenvalue
problem, not attempted. (b) no viscosity or vorticity confinement is
modeled explicitly; EDDY's own header discloses trying vorticity
confinement and finding it fights semi-Lagrangian advection's own
numerical noise -- this piece sidesteps that fight entirely by relying on
plain numerical diffusion, but a properly-tuned confinement force could
plausibly sustain the billows past this piece's own decay point instead
of letting them fade, not attempted. (c) THRESHOLD mode's symmetric
equal-density, equal-field-strength two-stream model is the algebraically
clean case Chandrasekhar's textbook gives in closed form; the real
granule/intergranular-lane boundary almost certainly has asymmetric
density and field strength on the two sides, which the general
(non-symmetric) Chandrasekhar formula could capture but this session did
not derive or verify. (d) GRANULE mode's "field strength" driving which
lanes reveal swirls is this session's own procedural random value, not
extracted from the paper (which was not read in full, sitting behind
Nature's login) -- an actual magnetogram-shaped field map, if the paper's
own figures become reachable to a future session, would replace this
piece's own arbitrary per-point noise with the real spatial correlation
between field strength and vortex occurrence the paper itself reports.
(e) no gravity/buoyancy term is in either mode's physics, despite real
granular convection being gravity-driven at heart -- ROLL mode's
Kelvin-Helmholtz layer and THRESHOLD mode's dispersion relation both
explicitly omit it, a disclosed simplification consistent with isolating
the shear mechanism specifically, not a claim that buoyancy is
unimportant to the real phenomenon.

---

## Piece 42: MURMURATION — A Flock of Elliptic Curves, Seen From Above (2026-08-10)
This session's web wander wasn't looking for number theory specifically --
it started from general "novel 2025/2026 discovery" searches (phonon
ballistic heat rays at room temperature; entanglement generated directly
from sunlight; a reversed-direction pulse in Earth's core) and landed,
via a search for AI-assisted mathematics, on MURMURATIONS OF ELLIPTIC
CURVES: in 2022, Yang-Hui He, Kyu-Hwan Lee, Thomas Oliver & Alexey
Pozdnyakov trained a classifier to guess an elliptic curve's rank from
its first 1000 Frobenius traces a_p(E), then noticed the pattern their
classifier was secretly exploiting (arXiv:2204.10140, Experimental
Mathematics 2024): average a_p(E)/sqrt(p) over every curve E in a fixed
conductor window with a given rank, plot the average against p, and a
striking oscillation appears, shaped differently for even- and odd-rank
curves. It is not a theorem -- eleven years after the individual-curve
Sato-Tate law was proved (Clozel, Harris, Shepherd-Barron & Taylor,
2011), nobody had noticed that POPULATIONS of curves do this. This
session's own web wander confirmed the topic is still actively worked in
2025/26: Dane Wachs's "BSD Invariants and Murmurations of Elliptic
Curves" (arXiv:2603.04604) mines 3,064,705 Cremona-database curves up to
conductor 500,000; "Murmurations of Elliptic Curves over Function
Fields" (arXiv:2603.13802) finds the same pattern over F_q(t); a third
2026 preprint extends it to modular-form coefficients at prime squares.
Checked against this gallery's own memory: no prior session had touched
this topic, and it is a genuinely different register from every earlier
piece here -- a STATISTICAL pattern across a POPULATION of number-
theoretic objects, discovered by a classifier noticing what it was
exploiting, distinct from BRUHAT's single-object combinatorics, ERDOS's
single-conjecture verification, or COULOMB/ULAM's individual random-
matrix objects.

This session has no LMFDB to mine -- every curve is generated from
scratch, live, in the browser, which forced a real mathematical choice
made honestly rather than hidden. The piece draws curves only from the
family y^2+y=x^3+Ax+B with SQUAREFREE discriminant Delta=-64A^3-
27(1+4B)^2, coprime to 6. Squarefree Delta forces Kodaira type I_1
(multiplicative reduction) at every bad prime, for ANY prime -- a basic
structural fact of Tate's algorithm -- so this restricted family gets
three things for free, in closed form, with no general implementation of
Tate's algorithm needed: (1) automatic global minimality (ord_p(Delta)=1
is far below the u^12 threshold non-minimality would require); (2) an
EXACT conductor N=|Delta|, no correction terms; (3) since every bad
prime is >=5 by construction, the classical Rohrlich criterion applies
cleanly with no p=2,3 special-casing (which is exactly where a naive
implementation would break, since the mod-p-square test it depends on
needs Hensel's lemma, which needs p odd): the local root number at a
multiplicative prime p is -1 if -c6 is a nonzero square mod p (split),
+1 if not (nonsplit); global w(E) = -1 times the product of local root
numbers over bad primes.

Root number is not rank -- actual rank needs descent or an L-function
computation this session did not attempt. But rank PARITY is provably
tied to the sign of w(E): the p-parity conjecture is a proved theorem
for E/Q (Dokchitser & Dokchitser, "Root numbers and parity of ranks of
elliptic curves," 2010), so splitting curves by root-number sign here is
a rigorous stand-in for the paper's own split by rank -- reaching
parity, not the exact rank value, honestly short of the original
construction in exactly that one way, the same register as BRUHAT's own
principal-ideal-only restriction relative to the 2026 paper's general
intervals.

Verified in Node before any canvas code (verify*.js, this session's
scratchpad, not committed) -- this session also installed PARI/GP 2.15.4
fresh via apt (not previously available in this environment) specifically
to have an independent, authoritative ground truth for elliptic-curve
arithmetic, the same role LMFDB plays for the published murmuration
papers this session couldn't reach data from directly: (1) N=|Delta| and
the Rohrlich root-number formula above, checked against PARI's
ellglobalred/ellrootno over EVERY one of 2888 curves from an exhaustive
small (A,B) sweep: zero mismatches. (2) this piece's own brute-force
point-counting formula for a_p(E) -- summing a Legendre symbol of
1+4(x^3+Ax+B) over every residue mod p, the direct #E(F_p) count, not a
closed form -- checked against PARI's ellap over 238 (curve,prime)
pairs: zero mismatches. (3) the individual-curve Sato-Tate distribution
was checked to visibly track the proved semicircle density
(2/pi)*sqrt(1-x^2) over 2260 primes up to 20000 for one sample curve
before committing to shipping it as SATOTATE mode. One real performance
bug caught by testing in an actual headless browser rather than trusting
Node timing: the naive apTrace() recomputed a full O(log p) modpow
Legendre-symbol test for every residue for every curve at a given prime,
fine in Node (~1-2s) but 52 SECONDS for the heaviest window in
Playwright/Chromium -- fixed by precomputing each prime's quadratic-
residue table ONCE (O(p), shared across every curve tested at that
prime) rather than recomputing per curve, cutting the worst case to
~3.5s; re-verified against the same 238 PARI pairs after the rewrite,
zero mismatches.

Three modes, X = conductor-window position (log scale, [X,16X), drag),
shared across all three: WAVE plots the murmuration itself -- average
a_p(E)/sqrt(p) for p=5..599, split by root-number class, Y drags a
0-8-prime smoothing radius; the window ranges from ~100 curves (small X,
visibly noisier) to ~7000 (large X, cleaner), with the true and possibly
subsampled count always shown live. FIELD shows the (A,B) parameter
plane itself, colored by root number, with the current window's curves
highlighted over a static pre-rendered backdrop of all 38,202 generated
curves; Y picks one curve from the window as a bright ring, carried into
SATOTATE. SATOTATE shows that one curve's own a_p/(2 sqrt p) histogram
against the proved semicircle density -- the individual-curve law
(2011, proved) sitting next to the population-level pattern (2022,
observed, unexplained) the other two modes show, the same "classical
proved theorem beside the open unexplained pattern" pairing BRUHAT used
for Tenner's 2007 theorem beside the 2026 AlphaEvolve record.

The honest gap, stated in the piece's own header and visible live in its
stats panel: this is a 2-parameter family, so its curve DENSITY at fixed
conductor is far below the true count of all elliptic curves of that
conductor (Wachs's own dataset has 3 million, this piece's sample sizes
range ~100-7000) -- genuinely noisier than the papers' own clean
averages, especially at the small-X end of the drag range. The
oscillation shown is real and independently computed, not looked up or
faked, but it is this session's own small, honest reproduction, not the
papers' dataset.

Open items for a future session: (a) an actual quadratic-twist family
(fixing one base curve and twisting by many squarefree d) would be far
denser than this session's 2-parameter (A,B) sweep and cheaper to
compute (a_p of a twist is just a_p of the base curve times a quadratic
character, no fresh point-count needed) -- this session explored it
(twist_check*.gp, scratchpad) but could not pin down a trustworthy
closed-form root-number-of-twist formula against PARI ground truth in
the time available, and dropped it rather than ship an unverified
formula; a future session with time to nail that formula (or to lean on
PARI's own ellrootno while precomputing a large twist family offline and
shipping the results as a static table) could reach LMFDB-scale sample
sizes. (b) actual rank, not just root-number parity, distinguishing rank
0 from rank 2 (both parity-even) the way the original paper's plots
sometimes do -- would need real descent or an L-function computation,
not attempted. (c) Wachs's own actual finding -- that BSD invariants
(Tamagawa product, real period, ...) modulate the murmuration's shape
without producing it -- is not touched at all; this piece only reaches
the root-number split the original 2022 paper used. (d) the function-
field analogue (arXiv:2603.13802) is a genuinely different arithmetic
setting (F_q(t) instead of Q) this piece does not attempt. (e) MAX_
WINDOW_CURVES=1600 subsampling is a real, disclosed cap, not silent --
but a genuine performance optimization (a typed-array-based batch
point-count, or a Web Worker so the heaviest window doesn't block the
main thread at all) would let a future session raise or remove it.

---

## Piece 41: BRUHAT — Permutations, Ordered By Complexity, Sometimes Snap Into a Cube (2026-08-09)
This session's web wander surfaced "Bruhat intervals that are large
hypercubes" (Jordan Ellenberg, Nicolas Libedinsky, David Plaza, Jose
Simental & Geordie Williamson, arXiv:2601.01235, January 2026): the BRUHAT
ORDER, the standard partial order on the symmetric group S_n coming from
the geometry of flag varieties (Ehresmann 1934; Chevalley 1958), sometimes
contains an INTERVAL [u,w] isomorphic as a poset to a literal
d-dimensional hypercube's vertex/edge structure. The authors weren't
hunting for such intervals -- they pointed AlphaEvolve (Google DeepMind's
evolutionary coding agent) at a different optimization in Bruhat-order
geometry, and it kept returning permutations whose intervals turned out to
be enormous hypercubes. For n a power of 2 they exhibit a hypercube of
dimension O(n log n), matching the largest possible dimension up to a
constant factor, with vertices given by what they call "dyadically
well-distributed" permutations, a digit-reversal-style property already
known from Monte Carlo integration and mathematical finance. This
session's WebFetch reached the abstract and secondary coverage (Institute
for Mathematics & Its Applications; Quomodocumque) but not the explicit
dyadic construction or a worked small example in closed form, so this
piece does not reproduce AlphaEvolve's own record-setting permutations --
it instead builds and independently verifies the classical machinery the
result sits on top of, and stays explicit throughout about the gap
between what it shows and what the 2026 paper claims.

Verified in Node before any canvas code (verify*.js, this session's
scratchpad, not committed): (1) the RANK-MATRIX Bruhat criterion used at
runtime -- u <= w iff rank_u(i,j) >= rank_w(i,j) for every i,j -- checked
against the textbook subword/reduced-word definition by brute force over
every pair of permutations in S_4 and S_5 (576 and 14400 pairs): zero
mismatches; (2) Tenner's 2007 theorem -- the principal order ideal [e,w]
is a Boolean lattice (a literal hypercube) iff w avoids the patterns 321
and 3412 -- checked against the actual interval size (|[e,w]| =
2^length(w)?) over every permutation in S_4, S_5 and S_6 (24, 120, 720
permutations): zero mismatches; (3) an explicit growing witness family
w_k = the (k+1)-cycle moving 1 rightward past 2,...,k+1 was confirmed to
be an EXACT hypercube graph (right vertex count 2^k, right edge count
k*2^(k-1), every vertex degree exactly k) for every k=1..n-1 at n=6 and
n=8 (up to a 128-vertex interval); its own reduced word s_1...s_k uses
each adjacent transposition exactly once, which is exactly why its length
can never exceed n-1 -- there are only n-1 of them; (4) the count of
Boolean permutations in S_n (13, 34, 89, 233, 610 for n=4..8, all
brute-force counted) matches the odd-indexed Fibonacci numbers F_7, F_9,
F_11, F_13, F_15 exactly; (5) a fixed non-Boolean permutation
(3,4,1,2,5,...,n), constant across n since it only acts on the first four
positions, has length 4 but interval size 14, not 2^4=16 -- rank-layer
profile 1,3,5,4,1, confirmed by brute force, visibly not the symmetric
binomial profile 1,4,6,4,1 a real hypercube would show.

One real bug caught before shipping: coordinatizing a verified hypercube
interval by bits, so CUBE mode could draw it as an actual cube rather than
just confirm its vertex count, needed two attempts. The first (label(v) =
OR of the labels of v's covering predecessors in the Hasse diagram)
collapsed every rank-1 vertex to the same label 0, since they all share
the single predecessor "identity" with nothing yet to distinguish them --
caught immediately by checking label uniqueness (128 vertices, 1 distinct
label) rather than trusting the construction on sight, not by eye. Fixed
by defining bit i of v's label directly from the order itself: 1 iff the
interval's i-th rank-1 element is <= v. Re-verified: 128 distinct labels
for the k=7 case, every Hasse edge differs by exactly one bit, and
popcount(label) equals rank for all 128 vertices.

Two knobs, shared by all three modes: X = n, the size of the permutation
group (4 to 8 -- S_8 has 40320 elements, checked exhaustively each time n
changes); Y = which permutation w to look at, from n+1 stops: identity
(dimension 0), the growing hypercube witnesses w_1,...,w_{n-1} (dimension
1 up to the verified maximum n-1), then the fixed non-Boolean permutation
for contrast. PERMS draws w as a wiring diagram (n points top and bottom,
line i to w(i)); a crossing is one unit of Bruhat length, and any instance
of the forbidden 321/3412 shape is highlighted in red when present.
INTERVAL draws the actual Hasse diagram of [e,w] -- every permutation
below w, layered by length, with a real edge for every covering relation
-- so the eye can compare a hypercube's symmetric binomial layer widths
(1,7,21,35,35,21,7,1 at the k=7 stop) against the lopsided layers of the
non-Boolean case (1,3,5,4,1) directly. CUBE draws a verified hypercube
interval as an actual d-dimensional cube (d coordinate axes at even
angles, the standard way to draw a hypercube past d=3, not a literal 3D
embedding) with a live SCALING readout: this session's own verified record
(dimension n-1, provably linear, since a Boolean permutation's reduced
word can't repeat a generator and S_n only has n-1 of them) plotted
against the 2026 paper's own claimed record for GENERAL (non-identity-
rooted) intervals, order n*log2(n) -- markedly bigger, drawn with an
explicitly illustrative, unextracted constant, and NOT reproduced by this
piece.

Open items for a future session: (a) the actual dyadically-well-
distributed permutation family and its explicit O(n log n) hypercube,
needing the paper's full body rather than its abstract and secondary
coverage; (b) general (non-principal, u != e) Bruhat intervals entirely --
every interval this piece draws is a principal order ideal [e,w], the
easy case Tenner's 2007 theorem already settles, not the harder general
case the 2026 paper's own record lives in; (c) the cluster-algebra /
Kazhdan-Lusztig consequences the paper derives from its hypercubes were
not touched at all; (d) CUBE mode's d-cube projection places all k axes at
fixed even angles with only a slow shared rotation -- a version letting a
visitor drag to reorient individual axes, or an alternate projection
(e.g. a genuine nested-Schlegel-diagram layout) was not attempted.

---

## Piece 40: BRAID — Two Eigenvalues, Tied Into a Knot (2026-08-08)
This session's web wander surfaced a Nature Physics pair from mid-2026: an
experimental paper, "Laser mode braiding on a chip" (an array of coupled
chip-scale semiconductor lasers, each pair sharing an EXCEPTIONAL POINT --
a degeneracy special to non-Hermitian, gain/loss systems where two
eigenvalues AND their eigenvectors coalesce into one, unlike an ordinary
Hermitian level crossing), and a same-issue News & Views by C. W. J. Koenig
& E. J. Bergholtz, "Braids of light," explaining why it matters: by
actively driving the chip's gain and detuning around closed loops in
parameter space, the authors watched the two lasers' complex frequencies
trace out literal braids -- normally invisible, since they live in the
complex eigenvalue plane, not real space -- and, closing the loops up,
genuine links and knots: Hopf links, trefoil knots, Solomon's links. The
general machinery behind this -- that a loop encircling an exceptional
point of a non-Hermitian Hamiltonian corresponds to an element of a braid
group, tying the eigenenergies into an actual geometric knot along the
path -- was established earlier by H. Hu, S. Zhu et al., "Knot topology of
exceptional point and non-Hermitian no-go theorem" (arXiv:2111.11346,
Phys. Rev. Research 4, L022064, 2022), cited here for the (2,N) torus-link
naming rather than re-derived from scratch. Neither the chip's real
multimode coupled-laser-rate-equation Hamiltonian nor its measured
gain/detuning trajectories reached this session in closed form; this piece
uses the textbook-minimal exactly-solvable EP Hamiltonian appearing
throughout this literature instead -- H(z)=[[0,1],[z,0]], eigenvalues
E_pm=+-sqrt(z) -- driven the way a real chip drives gain/detuning:
periodically around a circle z(theta)=d+R*e^{i theta} (R=1, offset d),
theta running 0 to 2*pi*N.

Verified in Node before any canvas code (verify.js, this session's
scratchpad, not committed), tracking sqrt(z(theta)) by continuously
unwrapping z's own argument rather than the principal branch: (1) for a
concentric loop (d=0) one full period swaps E_+ and E_- exactly (matched
to 1e-9, not just visually); (2) two periods return both eigenvalues to
their own identity exactly; (3) the number of times Re(E_+) crosses
Re(E_-) over N periods equals N exactly for N=1..5, matching the (2,N)
torus link's own crossing number; (4) whether E_+ returns to itself (even
N, two separate closed strands -- a LINK) or swaps into E_- (odd N, one
closed curve -- a KNOT) was confirmed directly from the continuously-
tracked endpoints, matching the standard gcd(2,N) component count; (5)
using the real physical drive (offset + fixed radius, repeated N periods,
not an already-multiply-wound circle) the total winding of z around the
origin came out to exactly N when d<R and exactly 0 when d>R for every
(d,N) pair tested, including d just 1% inside vs. just 1% outside R -- the
topological invariant is quantized and jumps discontinuously exactly at
d=R, confirmed numerically rather than only asserted.

One bug caught before shipping: the first version of the literal 3D BRAID
mode multiplied the already-pixel-converted height term by the shared
xy-scale a second time inside proj(), sending most of the curve tens of
thousands of pixels off-screen -- the on-screen remainder (only the
curve's first few in-range samples) looked like an innocuous straight
line with no crossings, not an obvious crash, and was caught by dumping
projected sy values directly via a headless Playwright + Chromium
screenshot pass (pre-installed in this environment), not by eye or by
reasoning about the code; fixed by removing the redundant multiply.

Three modes share one 2D drag (X = the loop's offset d from the EP in
units of its radius R -- 0 concentric, past 1 misses the EP entirely; Y =
target winding count N, 1 to 6). LOOP renders the actual drive: the
z-plane, the EP marked at the origin, the loop and a point sweeping it in
real time, ticking a winding counter only when the loop truly contains the
EP. BRAID renders the induced eigenvalue motion literally in 3D -- (Re E,
Im E, theta) as an actual spatial curve, two colored strands rendered with
a real rotating camera and depth-sorted (painter's algorithm) occlusion,
not a hand-picked over/under convention. SPECTRUM unrolls the same motion
into the flat 2D "braid diagram" style common in this literature -- Re(E)
against theta -- revealing a crossing once per period when the loop
encircles the EP, and, with no special-casing needed since it falls out of
the same formula, exactly zero crossings when it does not (a non-
encircling loop keeps Re(E_+) strictly positive and Re(E_-) strictly
negative throughout, so the two curves simply never touch).

Open items for a future session: (a) the real chip's own multi-band
coupled-laser Hamiltonian, actual measured gain/loss/detuning parameters,
and any reported linewidths were never obtained -- this piece's H(z) is a
standard textbook two-level EP model, not the paper's own specific
multimode system; (b) Hu et al. (2022)'s own "writhe" invariant (the
discriminant's winding, their proposed quantized topological charge) was
cited but never independently computed by this session against the
model's own eigenvalues; (c) only the simplest loop family (fixed radius,
real-axis offset) was explored -- a real chip (or a 3+-band system) can
drive arbitrary trajectories producing braid words outside the (2,N) torus
family entirely (non-torus knots/links), not attempted here; (d) BRAID
mode's camera auto-rotates on a fixed period independent of N or d, so a
viewer cannot deliberately orbit to inspect a specific crossing up close --
a drag-to-orbit control (distinct from the X/Y parameter drag already in
use) was not attempted, left for a future session with a spare interaction
axis.

---

## Piece 39: VACUUM — Empty Space, Forced Into a Crystal (2026-08-07)
On 5 Aug 2026 R. E. Stewart et al. published in Nature (arXiv:2509.19446,
first posted Sep 2025, this session's web wander surfacing it via the
techtimes.com/ATNF/quantumzeitgeist Aug-2026 coverage wave) the cleanest
evidence yet for a 1936-vintage QED prediction that had never been directly
confirmed: a strong enough magnetic field polarizes the QUANTUM VACUUM
itself into a birefringent medium -- two different refractive indices for
two photon polarizations, via virtual electron-positron pairs coupling to
the field, exactly as an ordinary crystal does except the "crystal" is
nothing at all. Their target, the radio-loud magnetar 1E 1547.0-5408
(surface dipole field ~5x10^14 G, ~11x the QED critical field
B_QED=4.4x10^13 G), showed IXPE+NICER+Parkes/Murriyang X-ray polarization
degrees up to 65-80% -- far above ordinary hot-surface emission models --
with the polarization angle locked to the star's magnetic geometry exactly
as vacuum birefringence requires. The Nature paper itself is an
observational campaign; it cites but does not reprint the underlying QED
closed forms, so this piece uses two from the actual paper trail those
citations point to -- J. S. Heyl, N. J. Shaviv & D. Lloyd 2003
(astro-ph/0302118, itself built on Heyl & Shaviv 2000 and Adler 1971),
fetched as a PDF and run through pdfminer.six (same broken system
cryptography/_cffi_backend import CLEAVE (session 35) hit; same
`pip install --force-reinstall cffi` fix worked again) since arXiv's own
HTML rendering dropped the actual equations' sub/superscripts: their Eq. 8
(birefringent precession-vector magnitude per unit path length,
|Omega|=(2/15)(alpha/4pi)(omega/c)(B_perp/B_QED)^2) and Eq. 9-10 (the
POLARIZATION-LIMITING RADIUS r_pl beyond which the vacuum can no longer
force polarization to track the rotating field direction, so it freezes:
r_pl=[(alpha/45)(nu/c)(mu/B_QED)^2 sin^2(alpha_incl)]^(1/5), numeric fit
~1.2x10^7 * mu30^0.4 * nu17^0.2 * sin(alpha_incl)^0.4 cm). A second,
independent 2026 paper on the very same source -- R. Taverna et al.
(arXiv:2601.15452, "The long quest for vacuum birefringence in magnetars")
-- supplied the rotating-vector-model formula and, notably, a RE-FIT
geometry (alpha~20 deg, zeta~75 deg) that the paper states excludes
Stewart et al.'s own preferred nearly-aligned geometry at >90% confidence;
this piece shows both, rather than picking a side (see PULSE mode below).

Verified in Node before any canvas code (verify.js, this session's
scratchpad, not committed): (1) the r_pl closed form's own published
numeric fit (1.2x10^7 cm at mu30=nu17=sinA=1) reproduced from the raw
algebraic formula to 2.3%; (2) for 1E1547's own quoted numbers (R=12km,
B_pole~5x10^14 G, 2 keV) the formula gives r_pl~159 stellar radii,
matching the ~100 R_NS the review literature quotes as typical for
magnetars (Heyl et al. 2003; Taverna et al. 2015); (3) the exact classical
dipole-field Cartesian vector matches the standard
(mu/r^3)*sqrt(1+3cos^2theta) magnitude formula to 1.9e-16 relative error;
(4) a photon confined to a single meridian plane (colatitude fixed along
the whole ray) shows EXACTLY zero field-direction rotation relative to a
fixed transverse frame, confirmed to 0.000 rad over 30 stellar radii --
since a static axisymmetric dipole has no azimuthal (B_phi) component,
only a genuinely skew (out-of-meridian-plane) ray sees the field direction
turn at all, which is why every ray in this piece is launched with a real
3D tilt rather than a purely radial one, and directly explains -- rather
than just asserts -- why Taverna et al.'s own re-fit geometry found a
NEGLIGIBLE birefringence effect on polarization degree (their fit puts the
emission region's rays close to this zero-rotation limit) while Stewart et
al.'s reading of the same data does not; (5) a direct RK4 integration of
the real Stokes-precession ODE from the stellar surface blows up to NaN,
because |Omega| falls as 1/r^6 and spans >15 orders of magnitude between
the surface and r_pl -- reproduced deliberately as a documented failure,
not hidden, since it is exactly why the literature uses an ADIABATIC-
THEOREM argument instead of brute-force integration; that freeze mechanism
was instead verified on a toy model with tractable numbers (idealized
Omega(l) with the same r^-6-style falloff and a rotating eigenaxis): while
the precession/rotation ratio Q>5 the tracked polarization angle follows
the rotating eigenaxis to within 0.075 rad, and once Q<0.2 the angle stops
changing (spread 1.8e-3 rad) while the eigenaxis itself keeps rotating,
unrelated to it -- confirming the freeze mechanism itself is genuine
physics, not merely asserted; (6) the rotating-vector-model formula
reduces to a constant PA for an aligned rotator (alpha~0) and sweeps the
full range for an edge-on geometry (alpha=zeta=90deg), both sanity-checked
numerically.

This piece therefore renders the ADIABATIC-LIMIT closed form directly
(polarization angle = the field's own transverse angle chi(r) for
r<r_pl, frozen at chi(r_pl) beyond) rather than a live per-frame ODE solve
of an equation verified above to be numerically intractable at real
magnetar field strengths -- an honest, deliberate substitution, not a
shortcut taken without checking the alternative first. One simplification
this costs: the real transition also generates a small circular-
polarization (S3) component as adiabaticity breaks down (noted explicitly
in Heyl et al. 2003); this piece's two-regime (track / frozen) model stays
exactly on the linear-polarization equator of the Poincare sphere and does
not show it -- see the open item below.

Three modes share one 2D drag (X = photon energy 1-10 keV in FIELD/
POINCARE, sets r_pl; Y = the ray's out-of-meridian tilt in FIELD/
POINCARE, sets how much the field direction actually rotates before
freezing -- dragging Y toward 0 reproduces verification item (4) live: an
aligned ray sees no rotation at all). FIELD renders the mechanism at the
star itself: dipole field lines under a log-compressed radial display
scale (an explicit, direction-preserving display-only remap, since r_pl
can run to 100+ stellar radii), a skew photon ray carrying small
polarization-ellipse glyphs that visibly rotate near the star and snap
"FROZEN" at the drawn r_pl shell, plus a faint field-strength-driven
"vacuum shimmer" of sparkle points. POINCARE shows the same ray's Stokes
state sweeping around the (S1,S2) equatorial disk as r grows, then
stopping dead at a glowing marker. PULSE reconstructs the actual
observable: the frozen polarization angle, spun through stellar rotation
via Stewart et al.'s own rotating-vector-model formula (their Eq. 1),
compared live against both papers' geometries -- Stewart's own qualitative
"nearly aligned" reading (illustrative small alpha, since neither fetched
source gave Stewart's fit numeric values explicitly) and Taverna's
quantitative re-fit (alpha~20deg, zeta~75deg) -- an honest live
disagreement between two 2026 papers on the same star, shown rather than
resolved.

Tested in a real headless browser (Playwright 1.56.1, this environment's
preinstalled Chromium via /opt/pw-browsers, no download): all three modes
screenshot-checked on 1280x800 desktop and 390x844 mobile-portrait
viewports, zero console/page errors, drag-to-set confirmed via the live
stat readout, and a corner-and-mode stress pass (all 4 drag-box corners
times all 3 modes) produced no errors or NaNs. Three real bugs caught only
by comparing screenshots against intent, not by reasoning about the code:
(a) r_pl first rendered as "0.2 R_NS" instead of ~150 -- the normalized-
unit version of the closed form had silently dropped a leftover factor of
R_NS_CM that the unit conversion from the real cm formula requires (traced
by cross-checking against verify.js's real-cm calculation, not spotted by
inspection); (b) the skew ray's tilt was originally built as
normalize(outward + tan(tilt)*yhat), which diverges as tilt->90deg and
made high-tilt rays collapse almost entirely into the screen-foreshortened
depth axis, visually clustering the polarization glyphs near the star
instead of spreading out toward the r_pl shell -- replaced with an exact
orthogonal-rotation formula (outward*cos(tilt)+yhat*sin(tilt), unit-length
automatically since the two vectors are exactly orthogonal for this
piece's fixed emission colatitude) and the exposed tilt range narrowed
from ~90deg to ~53deg max, since turns-before-freezing saturates well
before 90deg anyway (0.34 vs an asymptotic ~0.35) so nothing was lost; (c)
PULSE mode's rotating-vector-model curve was plotted unwrapped, so
position angle (only physically meaningful mod 180deg) overflowed the
plot box by 2x and drew a spurious diagonal "jump" line across the whole
panel at every wrap -- fixed by wrapping into (-90,90] degrees and
breaking the line (moveTo) at large jumps instead of connecting them.
- [ ] VACUUM: the two-regime (perfectly adiabatic / perfectly frozen)
      approximation used throughout stays exactly on the Poincare sphere's
      linear-polarization equator; the real transition near r_pl generates
      a small circular-polarization (S3) component as adiabaticity breaks
      down (Heyl et al. 2003's own stated result), not modeled here -- a
      future session could integrate the true Stokes ODE numerically
      through ONLY the narrow transition region near r_pl (tractable,
      unlike the full surface-to-r_pl range verified intractable this
      session) to recover that small S3 wobble and show it explicitly in
      POINCARE mode.
- [ ] VACUUM: FIELD/POINCARE fix the emission colatitude at a constant 35
      degrees and drag only the ray's out-of-meridian tilt; a full 2D
      (colatitude, tilt) drag surface, or GR-corrected ray paths near the
      stellar surface (this piece uses flat-space straight-line photon
      trajectories throughout, an explicitly disclosed simplification vs.
      the real ray-tracing literature uses), were not attempted.
- [ ] VACUUM: PULSE mode's "Stewart et al." preset uses an illustrative
      small-alpha value, not a number actually published by that paper (only
      fetched as qualitative "nearly aligned rotator" language) -- if a
      future session can reach the actual paper body (this session's
      WebFetch got the abstract/arXiv metadata page but not a fully
      equation-readable full text), replacing the illustrative value with
      Stewart et al.'s own fitted (alpha, zeta) would let PULSE mode show
      the real disagreement's actual magnitude, not just its existence.
- [ ] VACUUM: only a single fixed reference star (1E1547.0-5408's own
      quoted B_pole~5x10^14 G, R=12km) is modeled; a visitor-draggable
      B_pole or stellar radius, showing how r_pl and the freeze dynamics
      change across the broader magnetar population, was not attempted.
