bearing friction), solved EXACTLY each frame via its closed form w(t+dt) =
w_eq + (w(t)-w_eq)*exp(-B dt) — the literal analytic solution of a linear
ODE with piecewise-constant coefficients, not a numerical approximation.
FORWARD: torque(w) = mdot*R*(v_rel*sinB - w*R), visitor-draggable nozzle
angle B; the frictionless equilibrium is exactly v_rel*sinB/R, the classic
textbook fact that an unloaded sprinkler speeds up until water exits with
ZERO absolute tangential velocity (purely radial) — watch the ejected
droplets straighten as w climbs. SUCTION-NAIVE: the classical zero-torque
argument, r x v = 0 for any ray whose velocity points exactly at the
origin, non-interactive, fixed at delta=0. SUCTION-ASYMMETRIC: a visitor-
draggable perpendicular offset delta between each incoming jet's centerline
and the hub, giving each jet nonzero angular momentum mdot*v_rel*delta even
though it is still a straight-line ray — explicitly flagged as a small,
illustrative TOY geometry inspired by, not a reproduction of, the paper's
own measured internal flow field (no CFD or raw torque data from the study
was available to this session); its magnitude is calibrated only so the
marked default reproduces the reported ~50:1 forward:reverse ratio, not fit
to any specific published number.

### Verified in Node before any canvas code (verify.js, this session's
### scratchpad, not committed)
  1. RADIAL INFLOW HAS ZERO ANGULAR MOMENTUM. 20,000 sample rays with a
     deliberately LOPSIDED (not symmetric) angular distribution, each aimed
     exactly at the origin: max |r x v| over all samples was 3.5e-15 (float
     noise). Confirms the NAIVE mode's zero-torque prediction does not
     depend on any assumed symmetry of the intake — it is that a ray's
     position and antiparallel velocity vector have zero cross product,
     full stop, true of every individual ray regardless of how the
     collection of arrival directions is distributed.
  2. FORWARD ODE. RK4-integrated w(t) at three (beta, v_rel) pairs matched
     the closed-form equilibrium w_eq = mdot*R*v_rel*sinB/(mdot*R^2+c) to
     1e-4 relative error at t=20s simulated; the frictionless (c=0) limit
     matched the textbook v_rel*sinB/R to 1e-9.
  3. REVERSE ODE. Same check for the offset-jet model, RK4 vs closed form
     w_eq = mdot*v_rel*delta/(mdot*R^2+c), matched to 1e-4. (An earlier
     version of this model used bearing friction c as the ONLY brake for
     reverse, with no mdot*R^2 term — algebraically consistent but gave a
     required calibration delta of only ~10 microns / 0.01% of R to hit a
     1/50 ratio, an implausibly tiny number and a poor visual/narrative fit.
     Switching reverse to the SAME hydrodynamic brake term as forward, on
     the grounds that fluid still traverses the same physical arm of length
     R either direction, fixed this: the calibration delta became a much
     more legible 2% of R. A real modeling choice this session made and
     revised after checking the number it implied, not assumed correct on
     the first try.)
  4. RATIO CALIBRATION. At matched parameters (same mdot, v_rel, R, c;
     forward beta=90deg), w_eq,reverse/w_eq,forward collapses exactly to
     delta/(R*sinB) — verified algebraically and numerically to 1e-9.
     Solving for delta=0.02*R reproduces a 50.0:1 ratio exactly, confirming
     a "small compared to the arm" misalignment is consistent with the
     reported ~50x figure. Re-verified live in the browser (not just
     Node): loading SUCTION-ASYMMETRIC at its default (un-dragged) delta
     and letting it settle to equilibrium reads "forward:reverse = 50:1
     (reported ≈ 50:1)" in the on-screen stats panel — the same "measure
     the real statistic, don't just imply it visually" habit ARCTIC,
     PERCOLATE, ISING and KAKEYA (among others) each apply to their own
     critical/limiting statistic.

### Honest simplifications, disclosed on screen and in the header
The decorative looping arm shape (echoing the real study's "wild variety of
geometric loops and contours" test rigs) is stylistic, independent of the
physically-relevant scalar parameters (beta, delta) driving the verified
ODEs — the piece does not claim the drawn curve's own tangent direction is
the physical nozzle angle. The SUCTION-ASYMMETRIC offset delta is rendered
at 8x true scale (2% of R would otherwise be a couple of screen pixels),
labeled as such rather than left for a visitor to mistake for the real
proportion. Most importantly: the toy off-axis-collision model is this
session's own idealization of the paper's finding, built without access to
the study's raw CFD or torque-vs-geometry data — it reproduces the
REPORTED SCALE (roughly 50x slower) and the correct SIGN of the effect
(small misalignment -> small nonzero torque, matching "momentum flux, not
Mach's fluid-swirl theory, explains both directions"), not the paper's own
detailed internal flow field, which this session did not have.

### Rendering
Canvas 2D, single canvas (no offscreen-bitmap caching needed here — particle
counts stay in the low hundreds, nothing like BESICOVITCH's ~3000-leaf
overlap-fill cost). Two curved arms drawn each frame as a 24-segment
polyline; particles flow along the arm curve (hub-to-nozzle for FORWARD,
nozzle-to-hub for both SUCTION modes) and are drawn as small dots; on
ejection (FORWARD) or arrival from the ambient field (SUCTION), particles
switch to free straight-line flight with the physically-derived lab-frame
exit-velocity vector. A live sparkline (bottom-left, moved there after an
early version's top-right placement visually collided with the stats
text panel, caught by an actual screenshot rather than assumed safe from
the markup alone) plots omega(t) against a dashed line at the predicted
equilibrium so the ODE's own convergence is visible, not just asserted by
the final numeric readout.

## Piece 27: KAKEYA — The Needle Problem & Besicovitch/Perron Trees (2026-07-26)
Soichi Kakeya's 1917 question: what is the smallest-area plane figure in
which a unit-length needle can be rotated through every direction and
returned to its start? A circle (area pi/4) and an equilateral triangle
of height 1 (area sqrt(3)/3) both work; a three-cusped hypocycloid (area
pi/8) was assumed optimal for a decade. Besicovitch (1928) showed there is
in fact no minimum at all — a needle can sweep every direction while
confined to AREA ZERO in the limit — later simplified by Oskar Perron
(1928) into the "Perron tree": recursively bisect a thin triangle's
angular span and TRANSLATE the two halves so their footprints overlap.
Translating a shape never removes the directions of segments already
living inside it, so no direction is lost, but overlap lets the union's
area collapse far below the sum of the pieces. This is the gallery's
first piece in geometric measure theory / harmonic analysis rather than a
physical field, an agent system, a random matrix, or a tiling theorem.

Inspiration (this session's web wander): Hong Wang and Joshua Zahl's 2025
proof of the KAKEYA CONJECTURE in three dimensions — every 3D Kakeya set
must have full Hausdorff dimension 3, a 50-year-old open problem, and the
result behind Wang's 2026 Fields Medal (awarded alongside Gerd Faltings'
2026 Abel Prize the same season). The 3D theorem is a genuinely different
KIND of argument from Besicovitch/Perron's explicit construction: a
dimension bound proved via incidence-geometry/additive-combinatorics
machinery applying to EVERY Kakeya set at once ("sticky Kakeya sets"),
not a small-measure construction. There is no simple "3D Perron tree" to
render — the whole point of the 2025 proof is that reaching 3D needed a
fundamentally different, non-constructive technique. So this piece builds
the classical, explicit, fully-2D Besicovitch/Perron story that started
the field, and leaves the 3D theorem to prose and the backlog below.

Two modes: PERRON (a single wedge, apex fixed, spanning 180 degrees — the
full range needed since a segment's direction repeats every 180 degrees)
and BESICOVITCH (six independently-built Perron trees rotated around a
shared center to tile the full circle, echoing the classical picture of
combining rotated copies of one tree). A third, FAN, is the un-translated
"bush" baseline (a plain non-overlapping fan partition) for visual and
numeric comparison. Depth auto-grows over each mode's opening seconds;
overlap fraction is a visitor-draggable continuous parameter (horizontal
drag, this gallery's running "walk the parameter yourself" idea, ISING's
temperature onward through COULOMB's beta and ARCTIC's coin bias). A
needle continuously sweeps the covered angle range, its rendered position
found by locating whichever leaf currently owns that direction.

### Verified in Node before any canvas code (verify.js, this session's
### scratchpad, not committed)
  1. SANITY. With overlapFrac=0 (no translation, a plain fan/"bush"
     partition into 2^depth thin sub-wedges) measured area matched the
     exact sector formula theta/2 to 4 decimals at every depth tested
     (0 through 6) — a trivial re-partition changes nothing, as it must.
  2. AREA REDUCTION IS REAL BUT PLATEAUS. Translating each level's second
     child sideways by overlapFrac*sin(dTheta/2) — a rule this session
     derived itself, not a reproduction of Perron's own 1928 overlap
     bookkeeping — measurably shrinks area from the bush's 1.5708
     (theta=pi) down to ~0.44 by depth 9-10 at overlap 0.9-0.99, a real
     72% reduction. But it does NOT keep falling toward zero: pushing
     depth to 14 and overlap to 0.99 only reached ~0.457, with the curve
     visibly flattening well before that — an honestly-measured
     limitation of this session's SINGLE-AXIS, PAIRWISE-ONLY translation
     rule, not a property of the real theorem. A second translation rule
     tried (sliding perpendicular to each branch's own local bisector,
     rather than a fixed global axis) plateaued at a WORSE floor (~0.60
     at depth 14, overlap 0.9), an honest negative result reported
     rather than hidden. Perron's actual construction (not attempted
     exactly, given the complexity of reproducing its overlap bookkeeping
     with confidence in one session) provably reaches area as small as
     desired, at a famously slow rate.
  3. NEEDLE COVERAGE. For 5000 sampled angles spanning the full range,
     each angle's leaf was located and 5 points along its own unit needle
     were checked against that leaf's wedge: 5000/5000 verified. Close to
     a tautology (translating a wedge cannot remove the needle already
     inside it) but checked rather than assumed — the actual mechanism by
     which area can shrink with no direction ever lost. (A first version
     of this check failed 5000/5000 for an unrelated reason: sampling
     t=0, the apex itself, hits an r=0 direction-undefined degenerate
     point every time regardless of theta — fixed by treating r~0 as
     trivially inside rather than by touching the real construction.)
  4. DIMENSION VS AREA. Box-counting the FINITE-depth shape at four grid
     scales (0.02 down to 0.0025) gave a log-log slope of 1.99-2.01 at
     every depth tested (4, 6, 8) regardless of how far area had already
     shrunk — because any finite union of triangles with positive area
     trivially has box-counting dimension 2. That triviality IS the
     honest, illustrative point: area and dimension are different
     questions, which is exactly why "the area can be crushed to zero"
     (Besicovitch 1928) and "the dimension cannot be crushed at all, even
     in that zero-area limit" (Davies 1971 in the plane; Wang-Zahl 2025 in
     space) are two separate theorems, seventy years apart, not one
     theorem stated twice.

### Rendering
Canvas 2D. Leaves are drawn as one shared Path2D (apex + a short arc
polygon per leaf) for a single fill/stroke call rather than per-leaf
calls. A real, caught-only-by-measuring-actual-frame-rate performance bug:
filling/stroking that path fresh every frame — even with the Path2D
object itself cached — cost hundreds of milliseconds of real GPU/raster
time once BESICOVITCH mode's ~3000 overlapping leaves were on screen,
because canvas fill/stroke return control to JS before the compositor has
actually finished rasterizing a heavily overlapping semi-transparent path;
timing the JS calls directly showed near-zero cost while the ACTUAL
animation frame rate (measured via counted rAF callbacks) had collapsed to
2-3fps. Fixed by rendering the tree to an offscreen canvas only when its
parameters change, then blitting that bitmap every frame — settling to
47-60fps. A second, smaller perf fix: the initial area/dimension
rasterization used each leaf's full apex+-1 bounding SQUARE regardless of
its angular width, costing tens of millions of point-in-wedge checks at
depth 8+; switching to a tight per-leaf bounding box (sampled along the
leaf's own arc) cut the check count by ~10x while reproducing the exact
same measured area to 5 decimal places (verified side by side before
trusting the faster version). A third bug, caught only by a real
screenshot: the construction's own translation direction always slides
the second child toward -x, so the whole assembly visibly drifted off the
left edge of the canvas at high overlap/depth — fixed by recentering on
the leaf set's own bounding-box midpoint every frame rather than assuming
the root apex is a good visual center.

### A real, disclosed simplification the piece does not hide
Perron trees alone only shrink AREA — Besicovitch's actual continuously-
rotating needle also needs J. Pal's "joins", short connecting bridges
between neighbouring leaves so the needle can slide from one to the next
without a discontinuity. This build does not implement joins, so the
animated needle visibly JUMPS between leaves as it sweeps rather than
gliding smoothly — left on screen as an honest gap, not smoothed over
with an interpolation that would misrepresent what was actually built.

### Session Log entry filed below under 2026-07-26.

## Piece 26: ULAM — Random Permutations & the Tracy-Widom Laws (2026-07-25)
Stanislaw Ulam's 1961 problem: take a uniformly random permutation of
{1,...,N} and ask for the length of its Longest Increasing Subsequence
(LIS). The Robinson-Schensted-Knuth correspondence turns this into a
GROWTH process — inserting the permutation's values one at a time via row
insertion builds a Young diagram cell by cell, and Greene's theorem says
the diagram's first row IS the LIS — making RSK insertion a discrete,
combinatorial corner-growth / last-passage-percolation process, a member
of the same broad universality-class family COULOMB (session 25) closed
without finishing: COULOMB's own header explicitly flagged "a Tracy-Widom
mode... not attempted this session." Three exact theorems drive the piece:
Vershik-Kerov (1977) / Logan-Shepp (1977)'s deterministic arcsine-law limit
shape for the rescaled Young diagram boundary; Baik-Deift-Johansson (1999),
(L_N - 2*sqrt(N))/N^(1/6) -> Tracy-Widom GUE (F2) for random permutations;
and Baik-Rains (2001), the same statistic -> Tracy-Widom GOE (F1) for
random INVOLUTIONS (permutations equal to their own inverse) — a symmetry
constraint on a combinatorial object rerouting it to a different universal
law, echoing COULOMB's own beta-ensemble dial but pulled from pure
symmetric-vs-general combinatorics instead of a continuous coupling
constant. Two modes: PERMUTATION (GUE) and INVOLUTION (GOE), each growing
a live RSK Young diagram (rendered in Russian coordinates, boundary
overlaid against the exact Omega(u) curve) plus a live-accumulating
histogram of the rescaled LIS statistic over repeated fresh trials,
overlaid against the from-scratch Tracy-Widom density.

### Verified in Node before any canvas code (painleve.js, rsk.js,
### involution.js, verify.js — this session's scratchpad, not committed)
  1. F1 (GOE) and F2 (GUE) TRACY-WIDOM DISTRIBUTIONS COMPUTED FROM SCRATCH,
     not downloaded or hard-coded: the Hastings-McLeod solution of the
     Painleve II ODE q''=s*q+2*q^3 (defining property q(s)~Ai(s) as
     s->+inf) was shot backward via RK4 from s=12 using the Airy
     asymptotic series as the seed. Backward shooting is the numerically
     STABLE direction, but not unconditionally — nearby solutions of the
     same ODE have literal poles, and by testing progressively more
     negative targets (not assumed safe) the integration was found to blow
     up around s=-7 to -7.5 depending on the start point; sMin=-6.5 was
     chosen with real margin before that, an honest, visible truncation of
     the extreme left tail. F2(s)=exp(-int_s^inf (x-s)q(x)^2 dx) and
     F1(s)=sqrt(F2(s))*exp(-1/2 int_s^inf q(x) dx) (Tracy & Widom
     1994/1996) then reproduced the textbook mean/std to 4-5 significant
     figures: F2 mean -1.77109 (got -1.77109), std 0.90178 (got 0.90177);
     F1 mean -1.20653 (got -1.20653), std 1.26798 (got 1.26798).
  2. BAIK-DEIFT-JOHANSSON. Monte Carlo over random permutations (patience
     sorting, N up to 64,000, thousands of trials) confirmed LIS grows
     like 2*sqrt(N) with std scaling like N^(1/6); at N=30,000 the
     rescaled histogram's std (0.899) and skew (0.247) already closely
     match F2's own (0.902, 0.224), while the mean (-1.678 vs the true
     -1.771) is still visibly converging — a real, honestly-reported
     finite-N lag, not hidden by cherry-picking N.
  3. BAIK-RAINS. A uniform random INVOLUTION sampler was built from
     scratch via the standard recursive algorithm (the probability the
     largest remaining element is a fixed point, r(m)=1/(1+(m-1)*r(m-1)),
     kept in stable float form rather than the naive I(m)=I(m-1)+
     (m-1)*I(m-2) recursion, whose values overflow long before N=30,000).
     Sanity check before trusting anything downstream: sampled involutions
     of size 30,000 averaged ~170 fixed points across 8 draws, matching
     the predicted ~sqrt(N)=173.2. Those LIS statistics then gave rescaled
     mean -1.292, std 1.217 (true F1: -1.207, 1.268) — squarely GOE, not
     GUE (whose mean -1.771 is far off).
  4. A real dead end, left open rather than glossed over: fixed-point-free
     involutions were tried first (expecting Baik-Rains' GSE/beta=4 case)
     and abandoned once the rescaled statistic (mean -2.991, std 0.974)
     matched neither F1 nor F2 at any scaling this session could pin down
     with confidence, and deriving F4 from the F1/F2 Painleve data via the
     identity F4(s/2^(2/3)) = (F1(s)+F2(s)/F1(s))/2 gave mean -2.055/std
     0.640 — in the right direction (more negative, tighter, than GUE)
     but not a confirmed match either. The piece ships only the verified
     PERMUTATION/INVOLUTION pair; the fixed-point-free/GSE case is real
     unfinished business, not a silently-dropped feature.
  5. VERSHIK-KEROV / LOGAN-SHEPP. The full row-corner sequence (i,
     lambda_i) of a random Plancherel Young diagram, rescaled by
     1/sqrt(N), matched Omega(u) to mean absolute error 0.006 and max
     error 0.017 at N=20,000 (curve peak height ~1.273) — and because
     lambda_i is strictly decreasing in i, this single point sequence
     already traces the ENTIRE boundary curve (both its steep and shallow
     sides) with no separate conjugate-shape pass needed, the fact the
     piece's live boundary rendering relies on directly.
  6. A basic exchangeability fact checked by hand rather than assumed: any
     prefix of a uniform random permutation of size N is itself, in
     relative order, a uniform random permutation of the prefix's own
     size — meaning the piece's live growth animation (a single
     permutation generated once, inserted through RSK a chunk at a time)
     genuinely IS the Plancherel growth process at every intermediate
     frame, not merely a final answer revealed gradually.

### Rendering
Canvas 2D. The Young diagram boundary is drawn directly in the theorem's
own Russian coordinates (u,v) = ((lambda_i-i)/sqrt(N), (lambda_i+i)/sqrt(N))
— no separate rotation/projection step, since that IS the coordinate
system the row-corner sequence lives in. A real rendering surprise caught
by looking at the first screenshot, not assumed from the formula alone:
Omega(u) is NOT a mountain peak (this session's own header prose first
described it that way before the render corrected it) — it's a rounded
BASIN, flattened at u=0 relative to the empty partition's own sharp |u|
corner, since Omega(0)=4/pi~1.273 is a minimum, not a maximum, of the
curve. A second real layout bug caught only by an actual screenshot at
full N: the Omega curve's far-reaching V arms and the diagram's own fill
climbed high enough on screen to visually cross through the top-right
stats/histogram text, legible in a quick glance but not on close zoom —
fixed with an evenodd canvas clip carving out whichever screen region the
HTML stats overlay and histogram panel currently occupy (that region
itself computed once per frame and shared between the diagram-clip and
histogram-placement code, rather than two independent guesses that could
drift apart). A third, mobile-only bug from the same root cause: a narrow
portrait viewport put the top-right histogram panel directly under the
HTML stats text, both readable individually but overlapping into a mess
— fixed by branching layout() on aspect ratio (H > 1.05*W), relocating
the histogram panel to a full-width strip near the bottom and shrinking
the diagram accordingly, rather than only special-casing font sizes the
way the CSS media query alone had been doing.

### Session Log entry filed below under 2026-07-25.

## Piece 25: COULOMB — Eigenvalues as a Coulomb Gas (2026-07-24)
Every earlier field/agent piece here evolves a physical quantity — a
concentration, a spin, a velocity, a director, a flow. This one evolves
something more abstract: the EIGENVALUES of a large random matrix, which
Dyson showed (1962) behave exactly like a classical gas of charged
particles confined by a trap and repelling each other through the
Coulomb (log) potential — "Statistical Theory of the Energy Levels of
Complex Quantum Systems I-III". Real beta-ensembles (beta=1 GOE, beta=2
GUE, beta=4 GSE, "Dyson's threefold way") have joint eigenvalue density
p(x) ~ exp(-N*beta/4 * sum x_i^2) * prod_{i<j}|x_i-x_j|^beta; the matching
overdamped Langevin dynamics (Dyson Brownian motion) is simulated live,
N=324 "levels" on a line, with beta a CONTINUOUS parameter a visitor
drags 0 to 4 — the gallery's "walk the parameter yourself" idea (ISING's
temperature, PERCOLATE's p, ARCTIC's coin bias, MAELSTROM's Gamma)
applied for the first time to a symmetry class rather than a physical
constant, sweeping from beta=0 (no repulsion: plain independent Gaussian
particles) up through GOE/GUE/GSE live. A second mode (GINIBRE) runs the
complex-plane analogue — a 2D log-gas settling into the CIRCULAR LAW,
the semicircle's 2D sibling: eigenvalues filling a disk with perfectly
uniform density. A third mode (ZETA) is not a simulation at all: 2,500
REAL non-trivial zeros of the Riemann zeta function, downloaded this
session from Andrew Odlyzko's own published tables, whose spacing
statistics are the Montgomery-Odlyzko law — Hugh Montgomery's 1972 pair-
correlation calculation, recognized on sight by Freeman Dyson over tea at
the IAS as the GUE formula, confirmed on millions of real zeros by
Odlyzko (1987, 1989). Prime numbers and quantum-chaotic energy levels
sharing one statistic, for reasons still unproven (Hilbert-Polya).

### Verified in Node before any shader code (verify.js, checkbeta.js,
### checkbeta2.js, tunebeta_dir.js/2.js — not committed)
  1. SEMICIRCLE UNIVERSALITY. With confinement scaled as N*beta/4 (the
     textbook Gaussian-beta-ensemble normalization), the equilibrium edge
     radius is BOTH N-independent (2.02-2.19 across N=40..320 at beta=2)
     AND beta-independent (1.95-2.11 across beta=0.3..4 at N=250) —
     matching the textbook constant 2.0 to a few percent. A FIRST attempt
     used a beta-independent confinement -N*x_i and got the edge growing
     sharply with beta instead (0.78 at beta=0.3 to 3.2 at beta=4) — a
     real modeling bug (a missing factor of beta on the confinement,
     itself first written with a further missing factor of 2 on top),
     caught only by checking whether the claimed universality actually
     held rather than trusting the first plausible-looking run.
  2. SEMICIRCLE SHAPE. Pooling 25 post-equilibration snapshots (N=300,
     beta=2) against the theoretical rho(x)=(2/(pi*R^2))sqrt(R^2-x^2),
     R=2: R^2=0.908 across 24 bins.
  3. LOCAL SPACING / LEVEL REPULSION — a second, subtler bug. The dt safe
     for the GLOBAL confinement relaxation time (~1/(N*beta)) is far too
     coarse for the LOCAL pairwise-repulsion timescale near a close
     encounter: at dt=0.3/N every beta from 0 to 4 gave the SAME
     P(spacing<0.15)~=0.13, matching plain Poisson statistics almost
     exactly (1-e^-0.15=0.139) — the repulsion was being numerically
     destroyed every step, not physically absent, an easy thing to miss
     since nothing crashed and the macroscopic semicircle (claim 1) still
     checked out fine. Fixed by scaling dt inversely with beta; afterward
     P(s<0.15) drops monotonically 0.129/0.095/0.090/0.064/0.053 for
     beta=0/0.5/1/2/4 — real level repulsion. The shader mirrors this fix
     (per-substep dt shrinks with beta) but even so, LEVELS/GINIBRE's own
     LIVE histogram is visibly noisier than ZETA's precomputed one — an
     honest gap named in the piece's own header rather than hidden, since
     N=324 at 60fps can't match 25 pooled offline snapshots' statistics.
  4. GENERALIZED WIGNER SURMISE. The classic closed-form spacing laws
     (GOE/GUE/GSE) are special cases of one formula, P_beta(s) =
     C*s^beta*exp(-D*s^2) with D=(Gamma((beta+2)/2)/Gamma((beta+1)/2))^2,
     C fixed by normalization — verified via a from-scratch Lanczos Gamma
     function to reproduce all three closed forms to 5 decimal places,
     letting the live overlay curve be exact at every dragged beta rather
     than interpolated between three fixed cases.
  5. CIRCULAR LAW. The 2D log-gas's 95th-percentile radius lands within 3%
     of the predicted sqrt(N) (N=220), with equal-AREA annuli holding
     statistically flat particle counts (37,33,34,35,40,31) — the
     uniform-disk signature.
  6. THE REAL RESULT. Unfolding 100,000 real zeta zeros (downloaded this
     session from Odlyzko's zeta_tables) by the Riemann-von Mangoldt
     smooth density gives mean unfolded spacing 1.0000 and a GUE Wigner-
     surmise fit of R^2=0.9936 across 24 bins — the actual Montgomery-
     Odlyzko law, reproduced from real external data, not simulated;
     2,500 of these zeros are baked into the piece itself for ZETA mode.

### Rendering
WebGL1, one shared N=324 (18x18) position ping-pong texture pair reused
by two distinct SDE shaders (LEVELS 1D, GINIBRE 2D), both writing world-
space position into .xy so ONE shared accumulation-trail pipeline
(ATTRACTOR's own deposit/decay/render technique, reused near-verbatim)
draws either engine as glowing worldline streaks without knowing which
is running. LEVELS seeds directly at the semicircle law's own quantiles
(inverse-CDF, bisection) rather than a uniform lattice — the same "seed
near the model's own equilibrium, don't make it walk there" discipline
SWARMALATOR's header names — since a piece with only ~1 rendered-frame's
worth of real time per HOLD period cannot afford the ~2 rescaled time-
units a from-scratch reshuffle needs; only LOCAL spacing order, a faster
timescale, has to equilibrate live. ZETA mode runs no WebGL simulation at
all — a plain canvas 2D overlay draws the real baked zero positions and
the precomputed spacing histogram, left deliberately calmer/more static
than the two live sims, since its whole point is being the real, already-
finished answer standing next to the noisier live approximations.

### A rendering bug caught by screenshot, not by the console
The ZETA overlay draws a zoomed strip of real zero positions, THEN a
shared histogram-drawing helper that (for the LEVELS live case) needs to
fully clear the canvas each refresh. Calling that same helper from ZETA's
draw function wiped the strip that had just been drawn one call earlier
in the same frame — zero console errors, a silently blank strip, caught
only by a headless-Chromium screenshot (Playwright, this session's own
verification harness) rather than by anything that throws. Fixed by
making the clear an explicit caller-controlled flag instead of an
unconditional side effect.

### Interaction
Horizontal drag sets beta live in LEVELS mode (0 to 4, this gallery's
"walk the parameter" idea applied to a symmetry class); released, beta
resumes a slow automatic sweep across the same range so the full
POISSON-to-GSE story plays out even without a visitor's hand on it. A
clean tap reseeds GINIBRE's ring early; GINIBRE also self-reseeds every
15s regardless, replaying "filling the disk" the way NEMATIC's QUENCH
mode periodically replays coarsening. ZETA mode's drag scrubs which
stretch of the real critical line is shown.

### Honest gaps
LEVELS/GINIBRE's live spacing statistics are noticeably noisier than the
offline Node verification or ZETA's precomputed real data (see point 3
above) — named on-screen via the contrast between the two histogram
panels' fit quality, not hidden. WEIGHTED-style exact tangency formulas
for the semicircle/circular-law EDGE were not attempted here the way
ARCTIC eventually wants for its own ellipse; GSE (beta=4) has no matrix
construction as clean as GOE/GUE's real/complex Gaussian entries (it
needs quaternionic entries) and is only ever reached here as a log-gas
coupling constant, never built from an actual quaternionic random matrix.

### Inspiration
Web research into eigenvalue repulsion and Dyson Brownian motion surfaced
the Montgomery-Odlyzko story (a 1972 IAS tea-time conversation between a
number theorist and a physicist, confirmed on 8 million real zeros by
Odlyzko in 1989) as the session's actual hook — a genuinely new-to-this-
gallery domain (linear algebra / analytic number theory) with real,
downloadable, independently-obtained data to check a physics prediction
against, rather than another self-contained PDE or lattice model.

## Piece 24: MAELSTROM — A Hydrodynamic Aharonov-Bohm Effect (2026-07-22)
Web research this session (wandering from generic "novel physics visualization"
searches toward recent phys.org/arXiv coverage) surfaced a genuinely new-to-
this-gallery mechanism: Singh, Samuel, Liu, Angheluta, Concha & Bandi,
"Unified Hydrodynamic Analogue of Aharonov-Bohm and Lense-Thirring Effects"
(arXiv:2604.21543, 2026) — a draining-bathtub vortex's background flow acts
on surface waves the way a magnetic vector potential acts on a charged
quantum wave, producing (their own reported surprise) ROTATING nodal lines
in a standing-wave pattern, discovered first as an "artifact" in a real
water-tank experiment before the maths behind it was worked out. The root
phenomenon is older and well-established — Berry, Chambers, Large, Upstill &
Walmsley, "Wavefront dislocations in the Aharonov-Bohm effect and its water
wave analogue" (Eur. J. Phys. 1, 154, 1980) — but nothing in this gallery
before had touched a quantum-classical wave analogy, or a topological phase
effect that survives specifically because the causative field (here, flow
velocity) vanishes pointwise almost everywhere yet still shifts a wave that
never enters the region where it's nonzero. Distinct from NEMATIC's
topological defects (a homotopy-class fact about an order parameter) and
from EDDY's incompressible Navier-Stokes solve (fully nonlinear, self-
advecting) — MAELSTROM is a strictly LINEAR wave equation on a fixed
background flow, closer in spirit to HORIZON's photon geodesics (light/waves
moving through a fixed background field) than to any prior fluid piece.

### The governing equation, derived and verified before any shader code
A linear surface wave eta(x,y,t) advected by background flow U obeys the
standard convected wave equation (the same form used in analogue-gravity
"draining bathtub" acoustic black holes, Visser 1998): (d/dt+U.grad)^2 eta =
c^2*lap(eta). U is a Rankine vortex (solid-body core inside Rcore, ordinary
curl-free Gamma/(2*pi*r) azimuthal flow outside) — the standard point-vortex
regularization, chosen since every genuinely topological claim below depends
only on the far-field 1/r tail, not the core's exact shape. Two Node scripts
(maelstrom_verify.js, then maelstrom_ode_verify.js after the first's
2D-time-domain measurements proved too noisy to trust — see "a real bug,
twice" below) verified this session's actual physics claims:
  1. WAVE-SPEED SANITY (2D finite-difference, Gamma=0 control): a driven
     plane wave's measured front speed matched c to ~13%, the expected order
     of finite-difference dispersion error at this grid resolution.
  2. TOPOLOGICAL PHASE SHIFT. Separating the PDE in polar coordinates for
     one azimuthal harmonic eta=R(r)*exp(i(m*theta-omega*t)) gives an exact
     radial ODE (derived this session): c^2*[R''+R'/r-m^2*R/r^2] +
     Omega_eff(r)^2*R = 0, Omega_eff(r)=omega-m*Uphi(r)/r. Solved by shooting
     from a regular Bessel-series solution at r=0 (RK4 in r), the WKB phase-
     integral difference between a Gamma!=0 run and the Gamma=0 control
     SATURATES to a constant as the measurement radius grows from 250 to 500
     (agreeing to <0.05 rad, for m=1..3, Gamma=+-20/+-40) — the actual
     signature of an Aharonov-Bohm-like effect: the phase shift a wave picks
     up survives even though the causative flow has decayed to nothing at
     that radius. Confirmed to scale linearly in Gamma (2.00x Gamma gives a
     measured 1.99-2.02x shift) and to sign-flip with Gamma.
  3. ROTATION MECHANISM. PINWHEEL mode's rotating pattern is not scripted —
     it's a real normal-mode frequency-splitting effect, checked by root-
     finding the same radial ODE's Neumann (reflecting-wall) resonances for
     +m and -m separately: exactly degenerate at Gamma=0 (matched to 5
     decimal places), splitting symmetrically and oppositely once Gamma!=0
     (+0.00772 vs -0.00772 rad/t for Gamma=+-25, m=3) — the fluid analogue
     of Zeeman/Faraday splitting. A cos(m*theta) ring is an equal-weight
     +m/-m superposition, exactly what starts precessing once those two
     frequencies split; that ring is PINWHEEL's actual seed, uploaded once
     via a CPU-computed texture the same way EDDY's LEAPFROG mode seeds its
     verified point-vortex pair.

### A real bug, twice: measuring a small effect against a huge baseline
The FIRST verification attempt tried to measure the AB phase shift directly
in the 2D time-domain sim, comparing a Gamma-run's wavefront phase around a
circle to a Gamma=0 control. Two successive failures, both the same root
cause in different disguises: a driven plane wave's OWN phase already winds
many multiples of 2*pi around any circle wide enough to matter (kR~16 at the
parameters used), and naively unwrapping that raw phase — or even counting
raw wavefront zero-crossings — buries the small (order-1-radian) vortex
contribution inside noise and aliasing from the huge shared baseline;
worse, the first attempt also measured before the driven wave had even
finished propagating out to the sampling radius, since the domain lacked any
absorbing boundary and the run was stopped early to avoid wall reflections
contaminating the result. Fixing the boundary (a Rayleigh sponge layer) and
lengthening the run helped but never got path-independence or linearity
clean. The actual fix was to stop trying to extract the small signal from
noisy time-domain snapshots at all, and instead solve the radial ODE
directly (frequency-domain, no time integration, no noise) — the WKB
phase-integral and normal-mode approaches above, both of which the
2D time-domain approach was only ever an indirect, harder-to-trust proxy
for. An earlier hand-derived leading-order formula for the phase shift
(a small-Gamma Bessel-order-shift expansion) also turned out to be off by
a factor of several from what the full nonlinear ODE actually gives once
checked — named directly in this piece's own header comment rather than
quietly dropped, the same "an early attempt at a closed form didn't survive
contact with the real ODE" honesty this gallery's ARCTIC/PERCOLATE pieces
have each practiced for their own unproven or mis-estimated constants.

### Rendering and interaction
WebGL1, one RGBA ping-pong pair (eta in .x, psi=(d/dt+U.grad)eta in .y,
matching the verified first-order system) stepped by symplectic (semi-
implicit) Euler — psi first from the current eta, then eta from the new
psi — a single-pass-per-field scheme chosen specifically because it needs
no extra temp textures (unlike the full RK2 the offline verification used),
confirmed separately in Node to stay bounded and reproduce the same ~13%
wave-speed accuracy before trusting it in the shader. Three modes share one
solver: RIPPLES (periodic dropped impulses radiate out and visibly bend
past the vortex — Berry's dislocation phenomenon made literal and ongoing),
PLANE (continuous driven wave from one edge, the classic wavefront-past-an-
obstacle framing), PINWHEEL (the verified cos(m*theta) seed, reflecting
walls via CLAMP_TO_EDGE's implicit Neumann condition, m cyclable 2-5 by
tap). Horizontal drag sets circulation Gamma live (this gallery's standing
"let a visitor walk the parameter themselves" idea — ISING's temperature,
PERCOLATE's p, ARCTIC's coin bias — cheap here too, since U(x,y) is a plain
per-fragment formula, not a texture needing regeneration); a clean tap drops
a wave (or cycles m in PINWHEEL). A faint dashed ring at fixed radius drifts
at the vortex's own local angular velocity Uphi(r)/r as a literal, non-
decorative readout of the current flow.

### A tuning bug caught by watching the actual render
PLANE mode's first version looked wrong on screen well before any numbers
disagreed: after ~30s of continuous driving the whole canvas saturated into
a muddy, maxed-out tangle rather than legible curved wavefronts, because the
sponge boundary (tuned for a short, finite verification run) was nowhere
near strong enough to absorb reflections indefinitely, and a continuously-
driven linear wave equation with no other loss term has no reason to ever
stop accumulating energy. Fixed by substantially widening/strengthening the
sponge and adding a tiny global multiplicative loss each step (present in
every mode, including PINWHEEL, where it gently damps the standing pattern
by roughly half over one 30s mode hold rather than sustaining it forever) —
an honest, if inelegant, energy bound rather than a proper open-boundary
solve, caught only by actually watching a long-running screenshot, the same
"confirmed by reading back the actual render, not just the math" discipline
NEMATIC's own two real GLSL bugs needed.

### Honest gaps
The reflecting "tank" in PINWHEEL mode is this canvas's own square boundary,
not the circular tank the ODE analysis idealizes — real corner scattering
mixes a little azimuthal content between neighbouring m that a circular
boundary would keep clean, though the splitting mechanism itself (verified
above) doesn't depend on the tank's exact shape. No attempt was made to pin
the exact Gamma-to-phase-shift proportionality constant to a specific closed
form the way ARCTIC pinned its circle radius to n/sqrt(2) — only that the
shift exists, is genuinely topological (saturates with radius rather than
decaying), and scales the right way with Gamma and m; nailing the exact
constant (and understanding why the leading-order hand expansion missed it)
is left open. WEIGHTED-style continuous eta driving in PLANE mode has no
true open (non-reflecting, zero-residual) boundary, only a strong but finite
sponge plus a global decay term standing in for one.

## Piece 23: ARCTIC — Random Domino Tilings & the Arctic Circle (2026-07-21)
Inspiration this session came from wandering combinatorics/statistical-mechanics
corners of the web rather than another PDE or agent system: the Aztec diamond
AD_n (the diamond-shaped union of 2n(n+1) unit squares |x+0.5|+|y+0.5|<=n) has
an EXACT tiling count 2^(n(n+1)/2) (Elkies, Kuperberg, Larsen & Propp, "Alternating
sign matrices and domino tilings," 1992), and a uniformly random one of those
tilings has a genuinely startling property: near each of the diamond's four
corners the dominoes freeze into a single rigid brick pattern, and the boundary
between that frozen region and the mixed, disordered center converges, as n
grows, to an EXACT CIRCLE of radius n/sqrt(2) (Jockusch, Propp & Shor, "Random
domino tilings and the Arctic Circle theorem," 1998) — a sharp geometric law
falling out of nothing but uniform counting, no field, no force, no energy
functional. This is the gallery's first piece built entirely on discrete
combinatorial ENUMERATION rather than continuous PDEs (MEMBRANE/LENIA/EXCITABLE/
NEMATIC/SPINODAL), agent/force systems (PHYSARUM/SWARMALATOR/GYRUS/EDDY), or a
sampled Boltzmann distribution (ISING) — closest in spirit to PERCOLATE (session
20)'s "no dynamics, only combinatorics," but percolation's threshold is a
probability on an infinite line, not a literal circle traced on the actual
canvas boundary.

### The algorithm, and a real, instructive multi-hour bug
A uniform tiling can't be sampled by rejection (2^(n(n+1)/2) is already ~3.6e16
at n=10) — it is built via Elkies-Kuperberg-Larsen-Propp's "domino shuffling":
repeatedly (1) DESTROY every "bad" 2x2 block, one whose two parallel dominoes
point straight at each other; (2) SLIDE every survivor one cell in the
direction it already points (this is what carries it outside AD_n into
AD_{n+1}'s newly available ring); (3) fill the resulting holes — provably
always a clean disjoint union of 2x2 blocks — with a fresh horizontal-or-
vertical pair by independent fair coin. EKLP's own theorem: this three-step
shuffle turns a uniform tiling of AD_n into a uniform tiling of AD_{n+1}
EXACTLY, without ever considering the other 2^(n(n+1)/2)-1 alternatives.
Growing the diamond one order at a time IS the sampling algorithm.

This session's own aztec_verify.js (Node scratchpad, not committed — the
standing "verify closed-form math before any canvas code" discipline running
since HORIZON, applied for the first time to a discrete algorithm rather than
a differential equation) went through two wrong implementations before this
worked. The instructive one: assuming a domino's slide direction is a pure
function of its CURRENT absolute position, recomputed fresh from a checkerboard-
parity formula every generation. This is wrong in a way small cases actively
hide — a surviving domino's position parity flips every single time it slides
by one cell, so "recompute from position" silently reverses a domino's own
direction the instant it moves, and this stays invisible through the too-
symmetric AD_0->AD_1->AD_2 steps, surfacing only as a geometrically impossible
hole shape (a frozen "ring" that providably cannot be tiled by disjoint 2x2
blocks) at AD_2->AD_3 — three steps in, past what casual hand-checking of the
first step or two would ever catch. The fix: direction is a LABEL, assigned
once at creation (always in the pattern pointing away from its own hole) and
inherited unchanged through every future slide; badness is found by comparing
two CURRENT neighboring labels for a head-on collision, never by recomputing
either one from a position formula. Once fixed, the same script verified:
tiling validity through n=150 (uniform) and n=100 (biased, both p=0.15 and
p=0.85) — every cell of every intermediate AD_n covered exactly once, no
exceptions, well past this piece's own N_MAX=72; the exact count
2^(n(n+1)/2) against independent brute-force enumeration for n=1..3 (2, 8, 64,
all exact); uniformity of the shuffle's OWN output against that same brute-
force enumeration via chi-square (n=3: 25600 samples across 64 tilings,
chi2=55.16 against 63 degrees of freedom, squarely inside the range a genuinely
uniform sampler produces — repeated across 5 seeds at n=2 to confirm the first
run's chi2=14.94 was ordinary fluctuation, not a systematic bias); and, at
n=140, the measured frozen-temperate transition along the diamond's own
horizontal axis (~97 grid units) against the theorem's own predicted n/sqrt(2)
(~99.0) — within 2%, consistent with the theorem's own finite-size correction.

### WEIGHTED mode, and an honest limit
Propp's later "Generalized domino-shuffling" (2001) shows biasing the creation
coin (probability p of a horizontal fill instead of exactly 1/2) still samples
EXACTLY from the correspondingly reweighted measure, and Cohn, Elkies & Propp's
earlier "Local statistics for random domino tilings of the Aztec diamond"
(1996) proves the frozen/temperate boundary correspondingly deforms from a
circle into an ELLIPSE. This session verified the biased sampler is a valid
generalization (same validity check, p=0.15/0.85 through n=100) and the visible
elongation as p moves off 0.5 is the real, cited phenomenon — but WEIGHTED
deliberately draws no fitted ellipse curve, unlike UNIFORM's verified circle
overlay, because this session did not derive or verify the ellipse's exact
tangency-point formula; the same "state that no closed form was checked rather
than imply a false one" discipline PERCOLATE's own SQUARE SITE mode used for
its own unproven threshold.

### Rendering and interaction
Canvas 2D (not WebGL) — the first discrete-tiling piece since PERCOLATE/
SANDPILE/ISING to use it rather than a shader, appropriate since a domino
tiling is a literal finite rectangle set, not a continuous field. Growth is
animated directly (one shuffleStep per ~90ms), so watching the piece grow IS
watching the actual sampling algorithm run, not a separate visualization replay
of a precomputed result. Dominoes are colored by their persistent direction
label (4 hues, N/S/E/W) rather than by orientation alone, so the four frozen
corners each read as a single flat color and the temperate interior's genuine
four-way mixing is directly visible, not just implied. Interaction: drag
horizontally in WEIGHTED mode to set the creation coin's own bias and trigger
an immediate regrow at the new value — this mode's own instance of the "let a
visitor walk the parameter themselves" idea standing since ISING's temperature
(session 10), most recently reused by PERCOLATE's drag-to-p (session 20),
cheap here for the same reason it was cheap there: revisiting a different bias
is just re-running the same shuffle with a different coin, not a different
engine.

## Piece 22: NEMATIC — Liquid-Crystal Topological Defects & Schlieren Textures (2026-07-20)
A 2D nematic liquid crystal's director field n has head-tail symmetry
(n ~ -n, since it describes a rod-like molecule's AXIS, not a direction) —
the order-parameter space is a circle with opposite ends identified
(S^1/Z2), and its first homotopy group is Z with the peculiarity that
winding number m can be a HALF-integer (de Gennes & Prost, "The Physics of
Liquid Crystals"). This piece is the gallery's first field theory with a
topologically nontrivial order-parameter space: MEMBRANE/LENIA/EXCITABLE
diffuse ordinary scalars, ISING samples a discrete spin, but nothing here
before had defects whose very existence and classification is a homotopy-
group fact rather than a PDE detail. Implemented as a genuine 2D nematic
tensor order parameter Q=(Qx,Qy)=S*(cos2*theta,sin2*theta) (the standard
trick: Q has ordinary INTEGER winding k, and theta=k/2 is a half-integer
exactly when k is odd — this sidesteps ever representing theta directly,
which would need to handle the head-tail wraparound explicitly), evolved
by real time-dependent Ginzburg-Landau ("Model A") relaxation of a
one-elastic-constant Landau-de Gennes free energy:
  dQ/dt = D*Laplacian(Q) + a*Q - b*|Q|^2 Q
— i.e. ordinary reaction-diffusion, but of a 2-vector rather than a
scalar, making its point defects real topological vortices rather than
scalar hot/cold spots.

### Verified offline before any shader was written
verify.js (this scratchpad, Node, not committed — this gallery's standing
"verify closed-form math first" discipline applied to a defect field
theory for the first time) checked five separate claims against a plain
finite-difference port of the same equation: (1) winding number/total
charge is conserved under relaxation for an analytically-seeded +1/2,-1/2
pair (charge sum stayed exactly 0 across 40 relaxation steps); (2)
opposite-sign defects ATTRACT — separation shrank monotonically (20 to 17
grid units) over 450 steps and never once increased; (3) same-sign
defects do NOT annihilate — a +1/2,+1/2 pair stayed as two distinct
k=+1 cores through 150 steps, never merging to a higher-charge core or
canceling; (4) the real microscopist's diagnostic — a defect of charge m
shows exactly 4|m| dark "brushes" under crossed polarizers, and rotating
the analyzer turns a +m defect's brushes the SAME way and a -m defect's
brushes the OPPOSITE way — reproduced exactly from the analytic single-
defect solution (2 brushes for m=+-1/2, 4 for m=+-1, rotation sign
matching sign(m) in every case tested); (5) quenching from random noise
on a 96x96 grid gives a defect count trending 1086 to 362 to 120 to 57 to
37 to 28 across six checkpoints — consistent with the real ~1/t
topological-coarsening law (Yurke et al. 1993; Zapotocky et al. 1999):
predicted count at t=60 from the t=20 value via pure 1/t scaling is 120.7
against an actual 120, and at t=120 predicts 60.3 against an actual 57 —
a genuinely different coarsening MECHANISM from SPINODAL's own diffusive
t^(1/3) domain growth (session 17), since here it is discrete defect
motion and pairwise annihilation driving the coarsening, not a
conserved-order-parameter's bulk diffusion.

### Two real bugs the offline verification could not catch, found by
### the "verify closed-form math first" discipline's own blind spot
Both only appear once the SAME equation is ported to a GLSL fragment
shader on a real WebGL1 context, and both rendered as "nothing happens" —
worth recording exactly because a Node port of the same finite-difference
scheme is, by construction, immune to either:
  1. The simulation grid was originally 384x384. WebGL1 only guarantees
     REPEAT texture wrapping on POWER-OF-TWO textures; a non-POT texture
     wrapped with gl.REPEAT is undefined behaviour, and on this session's
     software (SwiftShader) renderer it silently sampled as pure black —
     so the very first simulation step multiplied the whole field by
     zero. Caught by reading back actual GPU texture state via
     gl.readPixels after a controlled number of manual simStep() calls
     (with requestAnimationFrame stubbed out via Playwright's
     addInitScript, to get a deterministic step count) rather than trusting
     a screenshot alone; fixed by moving to SIM=512, a power of two
     (matching every other ping-pong piece here, e.g. EXCITABLE).
  2. The Schlieren brightness formula pow(sin(x), 2.0) — GLSL ES's pow()
     is explicitly undefined for a negative base, and sin(x) is negative
     across half of any full period; on the same software renderer this
     produced a fully black frame despite a numerically healthy,
     non-uniform Q field underneath (confirmed via direct pixel readback:
     Q values varied widely in both magnitude and angle at the exact
     moment the screen showed uniform black). Fixed by computing the
     square explicitly (float s=sin(x); s*s) instead of calling pow() —
     the objectively more portable idiom regardless of this bug, not just
     a workaround for it. A pixel-exact check (compute the expected RGB
     from the CPU using the shader's own formula against the field state
     read back at the same texel, compare to the actual rendered canvas
     pixel at the corresponding screen coordinate) confirmed the fixed
     renderer matches the physics bit-for-bit, not just "looks plausible."

### Modes
QUENCH (default): noise-seeded seatch — periodically re-quenches from
random noise, showing the coarsening story repeat. LATTICE: a 4x4
checkerboard of PINNED defects (alternating +1/2,-1/2 so the periodic
domain's total charge is exactly zero, per Poincare-Hopf on a torus) —
echoes real micropatterned-anchoring experiments (a "Mosaics of
topological defects" paper turned up in this session's own research),
seeded from a near-zero (not noisy) background so order only ever
propagates outward from the pins themselves, with small +1/2/-1/2 marker
dots (from a live per-pixel winding-number computation, gated on the
loop's minimum order-parameter magnitude so the still-melted background
can't trigger false-positive markers from atan2's inherent sensitivity
near S=0). ANALYZER: two pinned defects with the polarizer continuously
auto-rotating (still user-draggable), so the brush-rotation sign rule is
demonstrated live rather than only assertable. PAIR: a single free
(unpinned) +1/2,-1/2 pair, seeded close together and with a faster
relaxation rate so the attraction-then-annihilation story plays out in a
real-time-watchable window.

### Interaction
Drag rotates the polarizer/analyzer angle (mapped across the polarizer's
own pi/2 physical periodicity, not a full turn) — the same real
diagnostic a microscopist performs by hand. A clean click/tap (movement
below a small threshold, to distinguish it from a drag) pins a new
defect at that point, alternating +1/2 and -1/2 each click so the
periodic domain's total charge always stays balanced; clicking an
existing pin removes it.

### Honest limitations
The pin-anchoring mechanism is a simplified stand-in for a real
colloidal/micropatterned inclusion — it locally blends Q toward the
isolated single-defect analytic profile rather than solving a proper
strong-anchoring boundary condition, adequate for the demonstration but
not a first-principles boundary-value solve the way LICHTENBERG's field
growth is. The LATTICE/ANALYZER presets' "near-zero background" trick
(seeding order only from the pins, to keep the charge-marker overlay
free of false positives) is itself only temporarily clean: Q=0 is an
unstable fixed point of the same Ginzburg-Landau equation that grows the
real defects, so given long enough uninterrupted viewing time, ordinary
floating-point roundoff eventually seeds its own visible coarsening
texture in the background too — an honest emergent consequence of the
same instability the piece is built on, not patched away, and kept in
check for ordinary viewing by each mode's own periodic reseed timer
rather than by suppressing the underlying instability. Integer (m=+-1)
defects, mentioned in the on-screen brush-count claim and exercised in
verify.js, never appear on-screen in any preset — real nematics usually
avoid them too (they typically split into two half-integer defects, a
real energetic effect this piece does not attempt to model).

## Piece 21: AXOLOTL — Growing Neural Cellular Automata (2026-07-18/19)
After Mordvintsev, Randazzo, Niklasson & Levin (2020), "Growing Neural
Cellular Automata" (distill.pub/2020/growing-ca). Every earlier field-based
piece here evolves a HAND-DESIGNED local rule: Gray-Scott's reaction
kinetics, Cahn-Hilliard's chemical potential, Stam's Navier-Stokes
discretization, Lenia's convolution kernel. This piece's rule was never
designed — it was TRAINED. A 16-channel cellular automaton (4 visible RGBA
channels + 12 hidden "communication" channels) updates every cell through
one shared neural network: a 48-dim local perception (each channel's own
value plus its Sobel-x/Sobel-y gradient) feeding a 48-32-16 two-layer
perceptron (2,096 weights), trained offline via hand-derived backprop
through unrolled CA steps — no numpy or torch were available in this
environment, so both the forward pass AND the full backward-through-time
pass were written from scratch in plain Node (nca.js/rollout.js/train.js,
this scratchpad, none of it committed — same convention as PERCOLATE's
perc_sim.js and every offline verification script before it) and checked
against finite-difference numerical gradients (60 sampled parameters
across all four weight tensors, both a deterministic fire-rate=1 case and
the real stochastic fire-rate=0.5 case, max relative error 3e-3) before
any real training run — this gallery's "verify before you build"
discipline applied to calculus instead of closed-form physics for the
first time.

### What was actually trained
Pool + damage-augmented training, Mordvintsev et al.'s own "Experiment 3"
recipe: a persistent pool of 16 32x32x16 grids, each iteration samples a
batch of 8, replaces whichever sampled grid currently has the highest loss
with a fresh single-seed grid, damages 1-2 of the REMAINING samples (a
random zeroed circle, ~70% of iterations) before a T-step rollout
(T uniform in [32,64]), and writes each rollout's own final state back
into the pool — so the network is trained not against "grow this pattern
once" but against grids at every stage from fresh seed through
long-persisted through recently-wounded, all under the same loss. Target:
a procedurally SDF-rasterized (signed-distance union of circles/ellipse/
capsules, smooth-min blended, antialiased) axolotl silhouette — head,
body, tapering tail, 4 leg nubs, 3-per-side gill nubs, one eye — in a
warm leucistic pink, premultiplied by alpha per the paper's own loss
convention. W2/b2 (the output layer) zero-initialized so the untrained
rule starts as a no-op (ds=0), the paper's own trick for early training
stability; W1/b1 small-random He-scaled. Adam (lr 1e-3 for the first 1500
iterations, 2e-4 after) with each of the four weight tensors' gradient
independently normalized by its own L2 norm before the optimizer step —
the official repo's own fix for BPTT gradient blowup across ~50 unrolled
steps, load-bearing here too: training was visibly unstable in early
smoke tests without it.

### Numbers, not just a picture that looked right
Ran in two background rounds (70min/2320 iters, then resumed to
60min/2081 more — 4,401 iterations total, ~130 minutes of real compute,
this session's whole training budget) on a 32-cell training grid matching
the inference grid exactly (no size-generalization gap to worry about).
Loss (pixel MSE over the 4 visible RGBA channels only, the same metric
the paper uses) fell from ~0.1-0.3 at initialization to an EMA of 0.0076
by the end of round two, with diminishing returns already visible (round
one: 10x reduction in 70min; round two: another 20% in 60min) — a
reasonable place to stop rather than a hard convergence criterion. Beyond
the training loss itself, this session verified the two properties that
actually matter for a piece with GROW/REGENERATE/FERAL/HIDDEN modes:
PERSISTENCE (loss stays in the same 0.0056-0.0071 band from T=64 out to
T=500 steps with no reseed — the pattern is a genuine stable attractor,
not something that grows once and then decays or explodes) and
REGENERATION (a large damage circle punched through the head/body at
T=120, then 80 more steps with no help: loss recovers to 0.0081, close to
the undamaged steady state, and the ASCII/pixel diff shows the hole
visibly closing rather than the rule just tolerating a permanent scar).

### Inference vs. training — a fully separate implementation
axolotl.html only ever RUNS the trained rule; it never trains. WebGL1 has
no multiple-render-targets, so the 16 channels live in 4 ping-ponged
RGBA32F texture pairs, and one CA step is two fragment-shader passes
(mirroring the trainer's own pre/post-update split exactly): pass A
computes perception+MLP+stochastic-fire into a raw unmasked new state (8
draw calls, one outGroup x per-group texture), pass B computes the
pre/post alive mask (3x3 max-pool of channel-3 on BOTH the input state
and pass A's raw output, thresholded at 0.1, ANDed together) and applies
it. Getting the weight-packing layout right mattered more than expected:
the shader packs W1/W2 into an RGBA32F texture in units of 4 (fetchW
returns a vec4), which only lines up with a plain row-major flat array if
each texel's 4 components map to 4 CONSECUTIVE hidden/output units — this
session reverse-engineered that layout from the existing shader (written
the prior session) by tracing fetchW's index arithmetic by hand before
writing a single line of the trainer, rather than guessing and finding
out via a garbled render. Verified via headless Playwright screenshots
across all four modes with zero console errors: GROW settles to ~34%
alive fraction; REGENERATE visibly heals a click/drag wound within the
same session's own screenshot pair; FERAL (multiple simultaneous seeds,
explicitly billed on-screen as "beyond its training") merges into one
large 82%-alive mass, a real extrapolation-failure demo rather than a
disguised second GROW; HIDDEN's false-colour view of channels 4/8/12
shows clearly differentiated red/yellow/magenta regions corresponding to
head, neck and tail — the model's own internal channels really did
specialize by body region, not stay uniform noise, the piece's central
thesis (Levin's "no cell aware of the whole shape, just a shared local
rule and neighbour communication") made visible without staging it.

### Honest limitations
At 32x32 with linear-filter upscaling the result reads as a soft,
glowing organic blob with a body and tail-like extensions rather than a
crisply legible axolotl portrait — the target's own finer features (4 leg
nubs, 6 gill nubs, one eye) do not resolve individually at this
resolution/blur, an inherent limit of the grid size chosen for real-time
WebGL1 inference, not a training failure (the SDF target itself, checked
via ASCII raster before any training, has all of them clearly separable
at the pixel level). A small persistent colour anomaly sits exactly at
the seed pixel in GROW/REGENERATE/FERAL — visible in every screenshot at
the same grid location, most likely a "scar" the pool-based training
schedule encouraged simply by revisiting that exact pixel across the
seed/persisted/damaged state distribution far more than any other single
cell; left as a genuine, mildly interesting trained artifact rather than
patched away, in the same spirit as HORIZON's "verify, don't guess" stance
on what's a bug versus what's real. 2,320+2,081 iterations is well short
of the originally planned 3,000 in the prior session's own WIP commit
message but landed past the point of diminishing loss returns; a third
round would very likely sharpen the silhouette's edges further but was
not run this session.


Broadbent & Hammersley (1957) posed percolation theory's founding question:
scatter edges (or sites) onto a lattice independently at random with
probability p — at what p does a cluster first span the whole system? This
is the gallery's first piece built on pure combinatorics rather than any
continuous dynamics: no field to diffuse, no boundary-value problem to
solve, no force to integrate, no Metropolis energy to sample against. What
grows on screen frame to frame is not physics evolving in time at all —
it's a fixed, already-decided random graph being REVEALED one edge at a
time in a random order, a visualization device, not a simulated process.
The only thing ever computed live is *connectivity*, via a weighted
union-find with path compression (Newman & Ziff 2001), the same algorithm
that makes a drag gesture able to jump p anywhere and rebuild the whole
graph from scratch in milliseconds — not by re-deciding any physics, just
by replaying a fixed random order up to a different point. Second Canvas2D
(no-WebGL) piece after GYRUS, for the same reason: union-find's inherently
sequential, non-embarrassingly-parallel structure has no natural GPU
shader mapping the way a diffusing field does.

### Four lattices, three of them with an EXACT threshold
A rarity in statistical mechanics, where most critical points (ISING's own
included, for anything but the special 2D square case) are only known
numerically:
- SQUARE BOND: p_c = 1/2 exactly (Kesten 1980, via the square lattice's
  own self-duality — the cleanest possible closed-form result to build a
  piece around)
- TRIANGULAR BOND: p_c = 2 sin(pi/18) ~= 0.34730 (Wierman 1981)
- HONEYCOMB BOND: p_c = 1 - 2 sin(pi/18) ~= 0.65270, the exact dual of the
  triangular threshold above
- SQUARE SITE: deliberately the honest counterpoint — no closed form is
  known at all for this one, only Newman & Ziff's own high-precision 2000
  numerical estimate (0.5927460); the mode's own on-screen label says so
  rather than implying a false equivalence with the other three
All four were verified offline first (perc_sim.js, this session's own
Newman-Ziff union-find port in plain Node — the "verify the closed-form
math before any canvas code" discipline HORIZON/SPINODAL/FLAME/EINSTEIN/
SPECTRE each used) before any of the geometry was ported into the piece:
the measured spanning-probability crossing landed within 1-2% of every
value above at N=96, 300 trials each. HONEYCOMB is rendered as the
standard "brick wall" representation (every horizontal bond kept, only
alternating-parity vertical bonds kept — a well-known graph-isomorphism to
the true 3-connected honeycomb lattice), not literal hexagon tiling — an
honest topological stand-in, not a geometric one, kept simple on purpose.

### SCALING: finally closing a gap flagged twice and left open
SANDPILE (session 8) and ISING (session 10) each noted in their own
backlog that a real power-law critical statistic was "implicit in the
visuals, not measured." SCALING mode freezes the square lattice exactly
at p_c=0.5 and live box-counts the largest cluster's fractal dimension
plus fits the cluster-size histogram's power-law slope against the exact
2D percolation universality-class values: D=91/48~=1.8958 and Fisher
exponent tau=187/91~=2.0549 (den Nijs 1979, via the Coulomb-gas mapping).
Offline verification (perc_scaling.js, N=1000, pooled over 8
realizations, box sizes restricted to a middle decade and the cluster
histogram restricted to a middle size-range — both ends are known
artifacts, lattice-scale discreteness at small s/box and finite-system
cutoff at large s/box) measured tau=2.046 (within 0.5%) and D=1.846 (about
2.6% low, a known artifact of box-counting a FREE-boundary sample-
spanning cluster at finite size, not a bug). The piece's own live readout
runs on a far smaller grid for real-time performance and is honestly
noisier still (observed D~1.8-1.9, tau~2.2-2.3 across repeated headless
runs) — flagged in-piece rather than dressed up as more precise than it
is.

### The interactive-parameter wish, finally cashed in
Nine earlier pieces flagged and left undone some version of "let a
visitor walk the parameter themselves" (ISING's temperature onward
through LICHTENBERG's eta, ATTRACTOR's (a,b,c,d), SOFTCELL's softness t,
EINSTEIN/SPECTRE's recursion depth, FLAME's xform editor, SPINODAL's
kappa, HORIZON's camera distance/FOV, EDDY's Reynolds-style dial).
PERCOLATE finally does it, for the cheapest possible reason: dragging
horizontally sets p directly, and because Newman-Ziff's whole design
point is that any earlier p is just a prefix of one fixed random order,
revisiting it is a full union-find rebuild from scratch (a few ms even at
this piece's ~20-30k edges) rather than needing any new engine.

### Verified via headless Playwright: FPS, all five modes, drag, resize
59-60fps sustained on Canvas2D/SwiftShader across all four bond/site
lattices' full sweeps and the SCALING mode's periodic re-randomization,
zero console errors beyond the usual benign favicon 404, drag-to-set-p
tested to land the exact target p (0.600 requested, 0.600 measured),
mode auto-cycling confirmed through all five modes in sequence, resize
mid-run (desktop -> narrow mobile -> wide desktop) with no crash, and a
390x844 mobile viewport with the bottom readout/hint panels kept legible
via a max-width media query (EDDY's own pattern, reused here).

### What's simplified, left honest
No literal hexagon tiling for HONEYCOMB (brick-wall stand-in, above);
SCALING's live D/tau are measured on a much smaller grid than the offline
pre-verification and are correspondingly noisier; the "growth" animation
sweeping p from 0 to 1 is pure visualization, not a claim that real
percolation processes evolve through increasing p over time (the physics
is a single frozen p, decided all at once — stated explicitly in the
header comment, since this cuts directly against every earlier piece's
own convention of dynamics being the whole point).

### Inspiration
Web research into percolation theory and the Newman-Ziff algorithm,
following up on a research thread ("unusual mathematical simulation
visualization... percolation") that surfaced percolation as a phase
transition genuinely unlike ISING's own thermal one — no energy, no
temperature, no stochastic dynamics deciding anything, just which edges
happen to be present in one fixed random graph. Chosen specifically
because three of its four natural lattice variants carry a rigorously
exact closed-form threshold (rare in statistical mechanics) and because
it let this session finally build the "measure the real critical
phenomenon" and "interactive parameter" ideas that SANDPILE, ISING, and
seven other pieces had each flagged and left undone across their own
backlogs — continuing the gallery's own established practice (SPECTRE,
FLAME and others) of building a flagged backlog item once the right
piece is on the table, rather than always chasing an entirely fresh
subject.

---

## Piece 19: EDDY — Incompressible Flow & Vortex Shedding (2026-07-16)
Every earlier field-based piece here diffuses a scalar or vector quantity
through a fixed LOCAL rule (Gray-Scott, FitzHugh-Nagumo, Cahn-Hilliard,
Laplace relaxation). This is the gallery's first genuinely harder equation:
incompressible Navier-Stokes, where velocity advects ITSELF (a non-linear
term nothing earlier here has) under a GLOBAL constraint — zero divergence
everywhere, enforced by a full Poisson solve every single frame, not a
local update at all. Found via web research into GPU stable-fluids
techniques (Stam 1999, Fedkiw/Stam/Jensen 2001, Harris' GPU Gems ch.38)
after noting the gallery had never done fluid dynamics despite 18 pieces
of reaction-diffusion, lattice, agent and field simulations — genuinely
new territory, not a variant of anything already built.

### The pipeline: Stam's stable fluids, one shared 320x160 grid
Per frame: add forces (inflow/buoyancy/a visitor's drag) + vorticity
confinement onto velocity -> self-advect velocity via semi-Lagrangian
backtrace (bilinearly resampled, unconditionally stable at any dt, which
is the whole reason a real-time GPU fluid sim is possible at all) -> a
small explicit diffusion pass -> compute divergence -> 28 Jacobi
iterations solving the pressure Poisson equation -> subtract the pressure
gradient (velocity is now divergence-free) -> advect a passive dye field
through that divergence-free velocity. Manual 4-tap bilerp() throughout
rather than gl.LINEAR (no OES_texture_float_linear dependency at all),
continuing HORIZON's own care about D3D9-era ANGLE portability.

### Two real bugs, both caught by watching the render, not the math
1. The Fedkiw/Stam/Jensen vorticity-confinement force is f=eps*h*(N x
   omega), h the grid spacing, included specifically so eps stays
   resolution-independent. Omitting h was this session's first working
   build: every mode dissolved into fine salt-and-pepper grid noise
   within a few seconds. The discrete grad(|curl|) term is two nested
   central differences, each its own 1/(2h) — without the compensating h
   factor it amplifies single-cell noise by close to 1/(4h^2) (several
   thousand times at this grid), and since confinement by design
   reinforces whatever looks like small-scale rotation, single-cell noise
   is exactly what it amplifies fastest — a runaway feedback loop.
   Isolated (not just fixed) by forcing eps to 0 and re-screenshotting:
   the wake stayed smooth and bounded with no grid noise at all, ruling
   out advection or the pressure projection as the actual source.
2. With h restored, KARMAN STREET (below) stayed stable while the three
   closed-box modes — no mean inflow to advect small perturbations off
   an open downstream edge the way KARMAN STREET's does — still slowly
   grew a checkerboard over 10-20+ seconds at the same eps. Confinement
   fighting semi-Lagrangian advection's own numerical dissipation is a
   real, narrower instability, not just a magnitude bug. Fixed by adding
   back the explicit viscosity/diffusion pass Stam's original algorithm
   has (a small blend toward each cell's 4-neighbour average — directly
   damps exactly the grid-Nyquist content confinement amplifies) and by
   making both eps and the diffusion coefficient genuinely PER-MODE:
   KARMAN STREET's strong inflow buys it far more margin than the three
   unforced modes, and LEAPFROG's much weaker point-vortex velocities
   needed their own gentler diffusion plus a render-only colour-gain
   boost besides — the SAME fixed damping fraction that safely controls
   noise at KARMAN STREET's scale was strong enough to erase LEAPFROG's
   own correct, much fainter motion within a couple of seconds. All of
   this was tuned empirically via a headless-Chromium/Playwright harness
   this session built specifically to screenshot and read back live
   energy values across parameter sweeps — the first piece in this
   gallery actually visually verified in a real browser before commit,
   rather than reasoned about from the math and shader source alone.

### KARMAN STREET: shedding measured, not just shown
Von Karman (1911) showed a cylinder's symmetric wake is unstable above a
critical Reynolds number and must break into an alternating vortex train.
Nothing here is scripted to alternate — the cylinder centre carries a
fixed 0.006 vertical offset (breaking the grid's own exact top/bottom
symmetry, since a perfectly symmetric discretisation can sit in the
unstable symmetric wake indefinitely) and the alternation emerges on its
own. A fixed probe cell downstream counts zero-crossings of its own
vertical velocity, in simulation steps (not wall-clock time, so the
measurement is independent of actual frame rate), turned into a live
Strouhal number St=fD/U — measured at 0.194-0.199 across repeated runs,
against the real ~0.2 plateau cylinder wakes hold from roughly Re~300 to
Re~2x10^5 (Williamson 1996). This solver has no explicit viscosity DIAL
(the diffusion pass is a fixed damping term, not a Reynolds-number knob),
so the ~0.2 match is an honest cross-check of the right order, not a
controlled replication of a specific Re — flagged plainly in the piece's
own header comment rather than oversold.

### LEAPFROG: Love's (1894) criterion, verified offline before any GLSL
Two coaxial vortex PAIRS (each a self-propelling +/- point-vortex dipole)
leapfrog — the trailing pair repeatedly overtakes and passes clean
through the leading one — whenever the diameter ratio alpha exceeds
3-2*sqrt(2)~=0.172, without the motion itself destabilising once alpha
clears a second threshold near (1/phi)^2~=0.382. Checked from scratch
first (leapfrog_sim.js, this session's own 4-point-vortex Biot-Savart RK4
integrator, the same "verify the closed-form math in plain JS before any
GLSL" discipline HORIZON/SPINODAL/FLAME/EINSTEIN/SPECTRE each used):
sweeping alpha from 0.10 to 0.70 showed small alpha just merges/separates
the pairs (one crossing, unbounded final separation) while several values
solidly above the golden-ratio-squared threshold gave dozens of bounded,
repeating crossings with no blow-up. alpha=0.42 was chosen for sitting in
that regime with margin either side (0.38 and 0.44 both proved fragile in
the same sweep). The seed itself is the closed-form desingularised
(Lamb-Oseen core) Biot-Savart field of exactly those four point vortices,
evaluated once on the CPU and uploaded straight into the velocity
texture — the verified offline math IS the initial condition, not just a
reference used to tune a separately-built shader effect. Honest limit:
the pairs visibly fade over the mode's ~30s hold rather than leapfrogging
indefinitely, this grid's own numerical dissipation, not scripted — not
unlike how even real viscous vortex rings only leapfrog finitely many
times before decaying (per "Leapfrogging of multiple coaxial viscous
vortex rings," cited in-piece).

### PLUME and STIR: the same confinement force, two other registers
PLUME's fixed nozzle injects dye and a small upward kick; density stands
in for temperature (the standard simplification) driving a buoyancy
force, and the identical vorticity-confinement force that keeps KARMAN
STREET's wake crisp is what rolls the rising plume's edges into paired
counter-rotating puffs. STIR lets a visitor's own drag inject velocity
and coloured dye directly via the same additive Gaussian-falloff splat
PLUME's nozzle uses, pointer-driven instead of fixed — this gallery's
now-standard "let a visitor perturb the field themselves" interaction,
here extended to injecting momentum directly rather than only reseeding
or stamping a scalar field.

### What's simplified, left honest
The cylinder and every wall are enforced by directly zeroing velocity
inside/at them each pass (immersed-boundary penalisation) rather than a
proper Neumann pressure condition at the solid boundary — the pressure
field inside the cylinder isn't physically meaningful, only the velocity
outside it, re-clamped every pass regardless of what a possibly-wrong
local pressure gradient there would otherwise do. The diffusion pass is a
single explicit blend-toward-neighbour-average, not an implicit viscosity
solve; dye shares the velocity/pressure grid rather than its own finer
one (most WebGL fluid demos give dye a higher-resolution grid than the
solve, per Pavel Dobryakov's WebGL-Fluid-Simulation); no 3D.

---

## Piece 18: HORIZON — A Real-Time Schwarzschild Black Hole (2026-07-15)
Schwarzschild's own 1916 exact solution to the field equations, rendered as
photon geodesics bent around a point mass — this session's answer to "what's
a genuinely different ALGORITHM," found via web research into gravitational-
lensing shaders (Riccardo Antonelli's and oseiskar's own black-hole raytracer
writeups) rather than another field/agent/lattice simulation. Every earlier
piece here evolves SOME substance through time on a fixed 2D orthographic
camera; this one has no simulated substance at all — every pixel casts a
single ray BACKWARD from a moving 3D camera through curved spacetime. First
piece with a literal camera, first with genuine 3D scene geometry, first
needing NO float texture / OES_texture_float extension at all (nothing
persists frame to frame — every frame is a pure function of the current
camera), and the gallery's first general-relativity physics.

### The physics: one ODE, not four coupled geodesic equations
Spherical symmetry means every photon's path lies in a single plane through
the black hole's centre, collapsing the full 4D geodesic integration to a
2D orbit problem. With u=1/r and rs=1 (world units fixed so the Schwarzschild
radius itself is the length unit):
    d²u/dφ² + u = 1.5 u²
— Newton's own inverse-square orbit equation (d²u/dφ²+u=GM/L²) plus exactly
one relativistic correction term. Two closed-form facts anchor the whole
piece: the unstable photon sphere at r=1.5 rs, and the critical impact
parameter b_crit=(3√3/2) rs≈2.598 rs — the sharp boundary between capture
and escape that becomes the visible shadow. Verified from scratch offline
before any GLSL (bh_sim.js through bh_sim5.js, this session's own scratch
directory — SPINODAL/FLAME/EINSTEIN/SPECTRE's "verify the closed-form math
