### 2026-08-07 — Session 39
- Added VACUUM: the empty space around a magnetar is not empty -- Stewart
  et al., "Vacuum birefringence and the polarized X-ray emission from a
  radio magnetar" (Nature, 5 Aug 2026; arXiv:2509.19446), IXPE+NICER+radio
  evidence that a strong enough magnetic field polarizes the QUANTUM VACUUM
  itself into a birefringent medium (a 1936 QED prediction, never before
  directly confirmed), surfaced via this session's web wander through
  techtimes.com/ATNF/quantumzeitgeist's Aug-2026 coverage. The observation
  paper cites but doesn't reprint the underlying closed forms, so the piece
  uses two from Heyl, Shaviv & Lloyd 2003 (astro-ph/0302118) instead: the
  birefringent precession-rate magnitude (their Eq. 8) and the
  polarization-limiting radius r_pl (their Eq. 9-10) beyond which the
  vacuum can no longer force a photon's polarization to track the rotating
  field direction, so it freezes. A second, independent 2026 re-analysis
  of the same star (Taverna et al., arXiv:2601.15452) supplied the
  rotating-vector-model formula and a geometry that the paper states
  excludes Stewart et al.'s own preferred fit at >90% confidence -- PULSE
  mode shows both rather than picking a side. See this session's own Piece
  39 entry above for full derivation and six-point verification detail,
  including a deliberately-reproduced failure (a direct RK4 integration of
  the real Stokes-precession ODE blows up to NaN, since |Omega| spans >15
  orders of magnitude between the stellar surface and r_pl -- exactly why
  the piece renders the literature's own adiabatic-limit closed form
  instead of a live per-frame ODE solve, and why that substitution is an
  honest, checked one rather than an unexamined shortcut) and a finding
  that explains rather than just asserts why Taverna's own re-fit found a
  negligible birefringence effect (their geometry sits close to this
  piece's own verified zero-rotation limit for meridional rays).
- Tested in a real headless browser (Playwright 1.56.1, environment's
  preinstalled Chromium, no download): all three modes screenshot-checked
  on 1280x800 desktop and 390x844 mobile-portrait viewports, zero console/
  page errors, a corner-times-mode stress pass (4 drag corners x 3 modes)
  produced no NaNs or errors, drag-to-set confirmed via the live stat
  readout. Three real bugs caught only by comparing screenshots against
  intent: r_pl initially rendered ~700x too small from a dropped unit-
  conversion factor (caught by cross-checking against verify.js's real-cm
  number, not by inspection); a tan(tilt)-based ray construction that
  diverged near 90deg and visually collapsed high-tilt rays into the
  screen's foreshortened depth axis, replaced with an exact orthogonal-
  rotation formula; and an unwrapped rotating-vector-model curve in PULSE
  mode that overflowed its plot box and drew a spurious diagonal line at
  every mod-180 wrap, fixed by wrapping and breaking the line at jumps.
- Inspiration: this session's web wander also surfaced a topologically-
  protected e/2-charge electron bound state from twisted light (Nature,
  early Aug 2026, dispersion-free ultrafast-electron manipulation) and
  continued particle-physics/quantum-proofs coverage from Quanta -- neither
  pursued in favour of the vacuum-birefringence result's freshness (Nature
  paper 2 days old relative to this session) and tightly closed-form
  literature trail.

### 2026-08-06 — Session 38
- Added ERDOS: Erdős's 1946 unit-distance conjecture, disproven in May
  2026 by an OpenAI general-purpose reasoning model's infinite CM-number-
  field construction (delta~6e-38), quickly sharpened to an explicit,
  checkable delta=0.014 by Princeton's Will Sawin and endorsed by Tim
  Gowers and Noga Alon -- surfaced via this session's web wander through
  Quanta/Gil Kalai's blog/the OpenAI writeup and arXiv:2605.20695. The
  disproof's own CM-tower construction has no small hand-computable
  example and wasn't reachable in explicit form from this session's
  WebFetch, so the piece instead renders Erdős's OWN exact 1946 mechanism
  (Gaussian-integer prime splitting, Cornacchia's algorithm, the Gauss/
  Jacobi two-squares formula r2(m)=4*2^k) at literal integer coordinates,
  plus an honest scoreboard of the real, still-open gap between Sawin's
  1.014 and the unbeaten 1984 Spencer-Szemeredi-Trotter 4/3 ceiling. See
  this session's own Piece 38 entry above for full derivation, five-point
  Node verification detail (r2(m) formula, Cornacchia's algorithm, the
  fast Gaussian-factorization method matching brute force set-for-set, grid
  embedding, and measured finite-grid boundary loss), and three real bugs
  a Playwright screenshot pass caught (a saturated single-alpha k=3 blob,
  visible banding from a naive thinning stride, and a click-to-advance
  handler that would have fired immediately after every drag-release).
- Tested in a real headless browser (Playwright): all three modes
  screenshot-checked on desktop and mobile-portrait viewports, zero
  console/page errors, drag-to-choose-k confirmed via the live stat
  readout.
- Inspiration: this session's web wander also surfaced continued AI-for-
  mathematics coverage (the Erdős-problems corpus as an AI benchmark,
  Fields Medal announcements for Yu Deng and John Pardon) -- not pursued
  as separate pieces, folded into ERDOS's own framing instead. The
  Compact Bonnet Pairs counterexample (flagged unaddressed for eight
  straight sessions through session 36) was addressed in session 37.

### 2026-08-05 — Session 37
- Added BONNET: one metric, two shapes -- Bobenko, Hoffmann &
  Sageman-Furnas, "Compact Bonnet Pairs: isometric tori with the same
  curvatures" (arXiv:2110.06335, Publ. Math. IHES 2025), the first
  compact counterexample to Bonnet's 1867 conjecture that metric + mean
  curvature pins down a compact surface, surfaced via this session's web
  wander through Jan-Apr 2026 press coverage (Quanta, ScienceDaily,
  phys.org, TUM). The paper's own discrete-differential-geometry
  construction (a Darboux transform of a discrete surface with planar
  curvature lines, closed up via an isothermic transform) was not
  reachable in explicit closed form from the abstract and secondary
  coverage this session's WebFetch could reach, so the piece implements
  instead the classical NON-compact ancestor of the same phenomenon: the
  associate (Bonnet) family of minimal surfaces joining the catenoid to
  the helicoid via the Weierstrass-Enneper representation, which shares
  the paper's exact two invariants (identical metric, identical H=0)
  across a continuously animating, globally different shape. Verified in
  Node before any shader code: E,F,G invariance to 6.5e-11, H=0 to
  3.1e-8, exact catenoid/helicoid endpoint identities, analytic-vs-
  numeric partial derivatives to <1e-4, Gaussian curvature matching the
  Liouville-formula prediction K=-1/cosh^4(v) to <1e-4, and a marked
  path's 3D embedded length matching its metric-only prediction to 3e-5
  across seven theta values. Three modes (FAMILY animating with a live
  E/G/F/H/path-length readout, TRACE as the bare curvature-line net,
  PAIR freezing the two named endpoints side by side) share one
  drag-to-orbit WebGL camera; tested visually via headless
  Playwright+swiftshader screenshots at desktop and mobile-portrait
  viewports, which caught and fixed a shader compile error (GLSL ES 1.00
  has no built-in cosh), an illegibly self-occluding two-turn rendering
  domain, and a PAIR-mode framing failure on narrow aspect ratios (fixed
  with an aspect-adaptive camera distance) before shipping.

### 2026-08-04 — Session 36
- Added ERGOSPHERE: extracting energy from a stationary ring by making it
  LOOK like it's spinning -- Nasari, Moussa, Kasahara, Thielens & Alu,
  "Observation of Floquet rotational super-radiance" (Nature, 8 July 2026,
  DOI 10.1038/s41586-026-10725-y, CUNY ASRC), the first clean lab
  observation of Zel'dovich's 1971 rotational-superradiance effect (the
  flat-spacetime sibling of Penrose's 1969 black-hole energy-extraction
  process), achieved with a stationary ring of electronic resonators
  time-modulated in a travelling pattern rather than any physically
  spinning part. Surfaced via ScienceDaily/EurekAlert's July 2026 coverage.
  The actual Nature article was paywalled (WebFetch hit a login redirect)
  and no arXiv preprint turned up, so this piece rederives and implements
  the general, model-independent Zel'dovich passivity argument from scratch
  rather than transcribing the paper's own circuit equations (never seen),
  with its own explicitly-disclosed, non-fitted absorptivity closed form
  for an actual number. Verified in Node before any shader code (oddness
  and boundedness of the absorptivity function, exact threshold crossing at
  omega=m*Omega, zero sign violations of the passivity argument across
  20,000 random samples, correct amplify/damp sign in 100% of 1680 swept
  cases). Three modes sharing one drag cursor (omega, Omega) and one
  auto-cycling azimuthal number m: RING (the mechanism -- 40 stationary
  segments, only their brightness pattern travels), GAIN (a live |R|^2
  heatmap with the amplification threshold drawn as an explicit line), and
  SCATTER (an incident cylindrical wave meeting the ring, its reflected
  rings visibly outgrowing the incoming ones in the superradiant regime).
  See this session's own Piece 36 entry above for full derivation and
  verification detail, including a real screenshot-caught bug (RING mode's
  first draft showed no visible lobe structure at all; fixed by rendering
  40 discrete segments instead of a continuous per-pixel gradient) and a
  real environment gap (the default pinned playwright@1.48 could not drive
  this environment's preinstalled Chromium at all; playwright@latest fixed
  it).
- Tested in a real headless browser (Playwright, upgraded to 1.62.1 this
  session after the pinned older version failed to launch the environment's
  preinstalled Chromium): all three modes screenshot-checked on 1280x800
  desktop and 390x844 mobile-portrait viewports, zero console/page errors,
  drag-to-set and tap-to-cycle both confirmed, and a frame-diff check (two
  screenshots 1.2s apart) confirmed RING mode's pattern is actually moving,
  not just time-varying in code without a visible effect.
- Inspiration: this session's web wander also surfaced a "magic"
  (non-stabilizerness) argument for holographic space-time's own pliability
  (Cao et al., via Quanta Magazine, June 2026) -- genuinely novel but judged
  too deep (tensor-network/holographic-code machinery) for a shader this
  session, left as a candidate for later. The Compact Bonnet Pairs
  counterexample to Bonnet's surface-rigidity theorem remains unaddressed a
  ninth consecutive session.

### 2026-08-03 — Session 35
- Added CLEAVE: truncate a photon with an optical shutter and get, not a
  smaller photon, but a superposition of photon numbers running to
  infinity -- Rukan, Gulla & Skaar, "A truncated photon" (Phys. Rev. Lett.
  137, 033601, 2026; arXiv:2510.21636, Univ. of Oslo), surfaced by this
  session's web wander via Science News' August 2026 coverage. Downloaded
  and read the actual paper PDF (WebFetch + pdfminer.six, after fixing a
  broken system cryptography/_cffi_backend import) rather than working
  from press summaries, since the general coverage states the headline
  result accurately but never the formulas. The paper's real derivation
  (a multimode Bogoliubov transformation with a singular Fourier kernel,
  Shale-Stinespring implementability, etc.) is far too deep for a shader;
  this piece instead uses exactly two of the paper's own closed-form
  results (Eq. 30's static shutter transmissivity Lorentzian; Eq. 29's
  upper bound on created-photon number vs. removal duration T, correctly
  diverging as T->0 and vanishing as T->infinity) plus, for the "swarm"
  itself, the standard exactly-solvable single-mode squeezed-vacuum photon-
  number distribution as an explicitly-labeled toy stand-in for the
  paper's own genuinely multimode state. Three modes sharing one 2D drag
  cursor (kappa0, and T on a log-mapped axis): SHUTTER (a spacetime
  diagram echoing the paper's own Fig. 1, incident/transmitted/reflected
  wave bands plus a diffuse violet "created-photon fog" scaled by <n>,
  swept by a repeating scanning "now" line); SPECTRUM (the static
  transmissivity as a real 2D (omega,kappa0) heatmap); SWARM (the photon-
  number bar chart, the session's own verify.js confirming along the way
  that P(0) is provably the single largest bar for any squeezing -- most
  truncations still produce nothing extra, even as the tail thickens).
  See this session's own Piece 35 entry above for full verification detail
  (two independent P(2n) formulas agreeing to machine precision, every
  distribution summing to exactly 1, kappa(t) continuity/monotonicity
  checked numerically, the linear-vs-quadratic bound crossover point
  matching the closed-form algebra).
- Tested in a real headless browser (Playwright installed fresh into this
  session's scratchpad, reusing the environment's preinstalled Chromium at
  /opt/pw-browsers rather than downloading a new one): all three modes
  screenshot-checked on 1280x800 desktop and 390x844 mobile-portrait
  viewports, zero console/page errors on either, tap-to-cycle and drag-to-
  set-(kappa0,T) both confirmed via the live stat readout. Two real issues
  caught only by reviewing actual screenshots, not by reading the shader:
  a test-side screenshot timing bug that landed just after the SHUTTER
  mode's 9-second sweep cycle wrapped (showing an almost-empty diagram --
  the piece's own behavior was correct, only the test's fixed 1-second
  delay was naive; fixed by polling elapsed time and shooting deliberately
  late in a cycle), and a real piece-side fix: SPECTRUM mode's first draft
  used a linear |T|^2-to-color map that read as nearly uniform red across
  most of the (omega,kappa0) range, replaced with a pow(T^2,0.35)
  perceptual-only remap after comparing a screenshot against intent.
- Inspiration: this session's web wander also surfaced a fractional Fermi
  sea realizing Haldane's 1991 generalized exclusion statistics in
  ultracold cesium (Univ. of Innsbruck, June 2026), an intermediate
  FCC/HCP nanoparticle superlattice structure (Brown Univ., May 2026), and
  -- via a search for the freshest chiral-optics inspiration -- the actual
  real-world nanofabricated realization of this gallery's own DIFFRACT
  piece's aperiodic-monotile chirality (Moritake et al., Nature Comms,
  July 2026, Univ. of Tokyo), confirming DIFFRACT's own underlying physics
  rather than being usable as fresh material a second time. None pursued
  in favour of CLEAVE's freshness and the paper's own tightly closed-form
  bound formulas. The Compact Bonnet Pairs counterexample remains
  unaddressed an eighth consecutive session.

### 2026-08-02 — Session 34
- Added TIMECRYSTAL: momentum bandgaps of a photonic time crystal (a medium
  whose refractive index is periodic in TIME rather than space), inspired
  by Guo et al.'s "Plasmonic metamaterial time crystal" (Nature, 29 July
  2026), three days old relative to this session. The gallery's first
  piece with NO simulation state at all -- every pixel of every mode is a
  closed-form function of (k, depth, wall-clock time), derived from an
  exact Hill/Meissner-equation solution rather than any field integrated
  forward in a texture. Three modes: SWEEP (oscilloscope-style sawtooth
  timebase showing a single dragged k's amplitude either ramping
  (momentum gap) or bounded (allowed band), reset every ~10s to stay
  inside a float32-safe exponent margin measured this session, not
  guessed), SPECTRUM (this system's momentum-gap map stacked against the
  ordinary spatial Kronig-Penney dual's frequency-gap map, same index
  contrast, side by side), TONGUES (the full 2D (k, depth) Ince-Strutt/
  Arnold-tongue stability chart, doubling as the shared drag control
  surface). See this session's own Piece 34 entry above for full
  verification detail (closed-form trace formula matched literal matrix
  multiplication to 9e-16, an RK4 integration of the ORIGINAL differential
  equation independently confirmed the matrix-predicted growth ratio to
  0.05%, and the exact Chebyshev-identity matrix-power formula needed for
  a scrubbable, non-accumulating shader matched repeated multiplication to
  9.4e-15) and two bugs this session's own verify.js and screenshots caught
  in themselves before shipping: a wrong U_{-2}=0 assumption that would
  have zeroed out every sweep's very first period, and an overreaching
  claim that the temporal and spatial gap formulas share a numeric axis
  (they share only the same closed-form recipe, not a literal alignment).

  all three modes screenshot-checked on 1280x800 desktop and 390x844

- Also changed the idle-vs-drag interaction model from what this session
  first wrote (every non-dragging frame overwrote the cursor with an
  auto-drift value, snapping back within a fraction of a second of
  releasing a drag) to a proper idle timeout (3.5s of no interaction, then
  a 2s eased hand-off into a continuously-advancing auto-drift phase so
  resuming never jumps) -- caught by re-reading the piece's own first
  Playwright drag test output before considering the interaction done,
  not assumed correct from the code alone.

### 2026-07-31 — Session 32
- Added DIFFRACT: the far-field (kinematic structure-factor) diffraction
  pattern of this gallery's own SPECTRE tiling, inspired by a July 2026
  Nature Communications paper that nanofabricated the real chiral aperiodic
  monotile and observed exactly this — sharp quasiperiodic Bragg peaks with
  a pinwheel-like, no-mirror-line chirality signature. Reuses SPECTRE's own
  ported tile grammar (Mat class, 9-category substitution rules) rather
  than re-deriving tiling geometry, the gallery's first piece to build on
  an earlier piece's own generative structure as input to a new physical
  calculation. Three modes: REAL (point cloud of 559 leaf centroids, drag
  sets illuminated count), BRAGG (chiral diffraction pattern of that
  subset), LATTICE (identical calculation on a periodic-lattice control,
  for direct contrast). See this session's own Piece 32 entry above for
  full verification detail (Friedel's-law sanity check, mirror-asymmetry
  measurement across the full aperture range — 52-79% chiral vs under 6%
  periodic-control — and a Node-verified Bragg-peak-sharpening trend with
  aperture size) and the WebGL1-loop-bound implementation choice (559
  literal `if(j<uCount)` terms unrolled into the generated shader source
  rather than a uniform array, to sidestep WebGL1's low guaranteed
  uniform-vector minimum on old/mobile GPUs).

### 2026-07-29 — Session 30
- Added SKYRMION: optical skyrmions arising from the 200-year-old Poisson/
  Arago spot (Fresnel diffraction of a plane wave around a small opaque
  disc), paired with an independently-constructed full Poincare beam whose
  Stokes-vector texture sweeps the Poincare sphere l times per frame and
  carries a live-computed, Berg-Luscher-lattice topological skyrmion
  number that stays pinned at exactly l under a continuous on-screen
  deformation. Three modes: POISSON, STOKES, SKYRMION. See this session's
  own Piece 30 entry above (this log entry itself was not filed at the
  time — backfilled by session 31 from that existing piece writeup rather
  than from fresh memory, so it is necessarily a summary of the recorded
  work, not a first-hand session note).
- Verified in Node before any shader code (verify.js/verify2.js/verify3.js,
  not committed): a discretized disc-diffraction integral matched the
  textbook closed form to <1e-4 and Poisson's own "bright spot" claim to
  5 decimal places; the vortex beam's phase wound by exactly 2*pi*l for
  l=1,2,3; the skyrmion number converged to l to <0.01% for l=2..5 on a
  polar (not uniform Cartesian) mesh, after a first uniform-grid attempt
  was caught drifting away from 1 as the grid was enlarged.
- A real bug caught only by rendering: the first Fresnel-integral prefactor
  was missing a factor of 1/i, rotating the whole diffraction pattern's
  phase by 90 degrees — invisible in intensity-only rendering but caught
  because it broke the algebraic identity U_disc = U0 - U_ap in the
  discretized-vs-closed-form check.

  standing backlog item.

### 2026-07-28 — Session 29

  from EDDY's classical viscous vortex shedding and MAELSTROM's single
  point-vortex phase singularity). Three modes: LATTICE (draggable Omega,
  rendered as the literal multi-vortex phase field, one atan2 term per
  lattice site), TKACHENKO (the same lattice under one shared transverse
  plane-wave displacement), KELVIN (five filaments, draggable forcing
  morphing a single low-k sway into a many-mode cascade).

  lattice point-count formula, Feynman's-rule areal density (<0.6% error,
  measured by direct counting, not just trusting the formula), phase-field
  Stokes'-theorem winding (matches 2*pi*enclosed to 1e-2 rad at 3 radii),
  Tkachenko wave periodicity/boundedness, and Kelvin dispersion sanity
  (real, positive, monotonic over the on-screen k-range). See this
  session's own Piece 29 entry above for full detail.

  faster in this session's own before/after measurement, the kind of

  (KAKEYA session 27, SPRINKLER session 28) rather than trusting code
